then\n  echo \"WARNING: upstream is on '$UPSTREAM_BRANCH', not 'main'\"\n  confirm \"Sync from '$UPSTREAM_BRANCH' anyway?\" || exit 1\nfi\n\nUPSTREAM_STATUS=\"$(cd \"$UPSTREAM\" && git status --porcelain)\"\nif [[ -n \"$UPSTREAM_STATUS\" ]]; then\n  echo \"WARNING: upstream has uncommitted changes:\"\n  echo \"$UPSTREAM_STATUS\" | sed 's/^/  /'\n  echo \"Sync will use working-tree state, not HEAD ($UPSTREAM_SHORT).\"\n  confirm \"Continue anyway?\" || exit 1\nfi\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Prepare destination (clone fork fresh, or use --local)\n# =============================================================================\n\nCLEANUP_DIR=\"\"\ncleanup() {\n  if [[ -n \"$CLEANUP_DIR\" ]]; then\n    rm -rf \"$CLEANUP_DIR\"\n  fi\n}\ntrap cleanup EXIT\n\nif [[ -n \"$LOCAL_CHECKOUT\" ]]; then\n  DEST_REPO=\"$(cd \"$LOCAL_CHECKOUT\" && pwd)\"\n  [[ -d \"$DEST_REPO/.git\" ]] || die \"--local path '$DEST_REPO' is not a git checkout\"\nelse\n  echo \"Cloning $FORK...\"\n  CLEANUP_DIR=\"$(mktemp -d)\"\n  DEST_REPO=\"$CLEANUP_DIR/openai-codex-plugins\"\n  gh repo clone \"$FORK\" \"$DEST_REPO\" >/dev/null\nfi\n\nDEST=\"$DEST_REPO/$DEST_REL\"\nPREVIEW_REPO=\"$DEST_REPO\"\nPREVIEW_DEST=\"$DEST\"\nSYNC_SOURCE=\"\"\n\noverlay_destination_paths() {\n  local repo=\"$1\"\n  local path\n  local source_path\n  local preview_path\n\n  while IFS= read -r -d '' path; do\n    source_path=\"$repo/$path\"\n    preview_path=\"$PREVIEW_REPO/$path\"\n\n    if [[ -e \"$source_path\" ]]; then\n      mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$preview_path\")\"\n      cp -R \"$source_path\" \"$preview_path\"\n    else\n      rm -rf \"$preview_path\"\n    fi\n  done\n}\n\ncopy_local_destination_overlay() {\n  overlay_destination_paths \"$DEST_REPO\" < <(\n    git -C \"$DEST_REPO\" diff --name-only -z -- \"$DEST_REL\"\n  )\n  overlay_destination_paths \"$DEST_REPO\" < <(\n    git -C \"$DEST_REPO\" diff --cached --name-only -z -- \"$DEST_REL\"\n  )\n  overlay_destination_paths \"$DEST_REPO\" < <(\n    git -C \"$DEST_REPO\" ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z -- \"$DEST_REL\"\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n#\n# Package the Superpowers Codex plugin as a rootless archive for portal upload.\n#\n# The Codex portal artifact differs from the old openai/plugins sync flow:\n# it is a standalone archive, but it still needs the OpenAI-owned\n# skills/*/agents/openai.yaml metadata that used to be preserved from the\n# destination plugin repo. Seed that metadata from a prior official package.\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/..\" && pwd)\"\n\nREF=\"HEAD\"\nOUTPUT=\"\"\nFORMAT=\"\"\nMETADATA_SOURCE=\"\"\nALLOW_DIRTY=0\nKEEP_STAGE=0\n\nusage() {\n  cat <<'EOF'\nUsage:\n  scripts/package-codex-plugin.sh [options]\n\nOptions:\n  --output PATH            Write archive to PATH.\n                           Default: ../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers-VERSION.zip\n  --format FORMAT          Archive format: zip or tar.gz. Default: zip.\n                           If --output ends in .zip, .tar.gz, or .tgz, that\n                           extension is used when --format is omitted.\n  --metadata-source PATH   Prior official package directory, .zip, or .tar.gz used to\n                           seed skills/*/agents/openai.yaml.\n                           Default: ../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers,\n                           falling back to superpowers.zip, then superpowers.tar.gz\n  --ref REF                Git ref to package. Default: HEAD.\n  --allow-dirty            Permit a dirty working tree. The archive still uses --ref.\n  --keep-stage             Print and keep the temporary staging directory.\n  -h, --help               Show this help.\n\nThe archive is rootless: .codex-plugin/, assets/, skills/, README.md, LICENSE,\nand CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md sit at the archive root. Source-only repo files, hooks, tests,\ndocs, and other harness manifests are intentionally not shipped.\nEOF\n}\n\ndie() {\n  echo \"ERROR: $*\" >&2\n  exit 1\n}\n\nwhile [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do\n  case \"$1\" in\n    --output)\n      [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die \"--output requires a path\"\n      OUTPUT=\"$2\"\n      shift 2\n      ;;\n    --format)\n      [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die \"--format requires a value\"\n      case \"$2\" in\n        zip)\n          FORMAT=\"zip\"\n          ;;\n        tar.gz|tgz)\n          FORMAT=\"tar.gz\"\n          ;;\n        *)\n          die \"--format must be zip or tar.gz\"\n          ;;\n      esac\n      shift 2\n      ;;\n    --metadata-source)\n      [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die \"--metadata-source requires a path\"\n      METADATA_SOURCE=\"$2\"\n      shift 2\n      ;;\n    --ref)\n      [[ $# -ge 2 ]] || die \"--ref requires a value\"\n      REF=\"$2\"\n      shift 2\n      ;;\n    --allow-dirty)\n      ALLOW_DIRTY=1\n      shift\n      ;;\n    --keep-stage)\n      KEEP_STAGE=1\n      shift\n      ;;\n    -h|--help)\n      usage\n      exit 0\n      ;;\n    *)\n      echo \"Unknown arg: $1\" >&2\n      usage >&2\n      exit 2\n      ;;\n  esac\ndone\n\ninfer_format_from_output() {\n  local output_path=\"$1\"\n\n  case \"$output_path\" in\n    *.tar.gz|*.tgz)\n      printf '%s\\n' \"tar.gz\"\n      ;;\n    *.zip)\n      printf '%s\\n' \"zip\"\n      ;;\n    *)\n      return 1\n      ;;\n  esac\n}\n\nif [[ -z \"$FORMAT\" ]]; then\n  FORMAT=\"$(infer_format_from_output \"$OUTPUT\" || true)\"\n  if [[ -z \"$FORMAT\" ]]; then\n    FORMAT=\"zip\"\n  fi\nelse\n  output_format=\"$(infer_format_from_output \"$OUTPUT\" || true)\"\n  if [[ -n \"$output_format\" && \"$output_format\" != \"$FORMAT\" ]]; then\n    die \"--output extension does not match --format $FORMAT: $OUTPUT\"\n  fi\nfi\n\ncommand -v git >/dev/null || die \"git not found in PATH\"\ncommand -v jq >/dev/null || die \"jq not found in PATH\"\ncommand -v tar >/dev/null || die \"tar not found in PATH\"\ncommand -v gzip >/dev/null || die \"gzip not found in PATH\"\ncommand -v shasum >/dev/null || die \"shasum not found in PATH\"\nif [[ \"$FORMAT\" == \"zip\" ]]; then\n  command -v zip >/dev/null || die \"zip not found in PATH\"\n  command -v unzip >/dev/null || die \"unzip not found in PATH\"\nfi\n\n[[ -d \"$REPO_ROOT/.git\" ]] || die \"repo root is not a git checkout: $REPO_ROOT\"\ngit -C \"$REPO_ROOT\" rev-parse --verify \"$REF^{commit}\" >/dev/null ||\n  die \"git ref does not resolve to a commit: $REF\"\n\nif [[ \"$ALLOW_DIRTY\" -ne 1 ]]; then\n  dirty_status=\"$(git -C \"$REPO_ROOT\" status --porcelain --untracked-files=all)\"\n  if [[ -n \"$dirty_status\" ]]; then\n    echo \"Working tree has uncommitted changes:\" >&2\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$dirty_status\" | sed 's/^/  /' >&2\n    die \"commit or stash changes first, or pass --allow-dirty to package $REF anyway\"\n  fi\nfi\n\nif [[ -z \"$METADATA_SOURCE\" ]]; then\n  if [[ -d \"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers\" ]]; then\n    METADATA_SOURCE=\"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers\"\n  elif [[ -f \"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers.zip\" ]]; then\n    METADATA_SOURCE=\"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers.zip\"\n  elif [[ -f \"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers.tar.gz\" ]]; then\n    METADATA_SOURCE=\"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers.tar.gz\"\n  else\n    die \"no metadata source found; pass --metadata-source <prior package dir, zip, or tar.gz>\"\n  fi\nfi\n\nWORK_DIR=\"$(mktemp -d \"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/superpowers-codex-package.XXXXXX\")\"\nSTAGE=\"$WORK_DIR/payload\"\nMETADATA_WORK=\"$WORK_DIR/metadata\"\nARCHIVE_LIST=\"$WORK_DIR/archive-list\"\n\ncleanup() {\n  if [[ \"$KEEP_STAGE\" -eq 1 ]]; then\n    echo \"Keeping staging directory: $WORK_DIR\" >&2\n  else\n    rm -rf \"$WORK_DIR\"\n  fi\n}\ntrap cleanup EXIT\n\nmkdir -p \"$STAGE\" \"$METADATA_WORK\"\n\nmetadata_root_from_dir() {\n  local candidate=\"$1\"\n  local nested\n\n  if [[ -d \"$candidate/skills\" ]]; then\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$candidate\"\n    return 0\n  fi\n\n  nested=\"$(find \"$candidate\" -mindepth 2 -maxdepth 2 -type d -name skills -print -quit)\"\n  if [[ -n \"$nested\" ]]; then\n    dirname \"$nested\"\n    return 0\n  fi\n\n  return 1\n}\n\nprepare_metadata_root() {\n  local source=\"$1\"\n  local root\n\n  if [[ -d \"$source\" ]]; then\n    root=\"$(cd \"$source\" && pwd)\"\n  elif [[ -f \"$source\" ]]; then\n    case \"$source\" in\n      *.tar.gz|*.tgz)\n        tar -xzf \"$source\" -C \"$METADATA_WORK\"\n        root=\"$METADATA_WORK\"\n        ;;\n      *.zip)\n        command -v unzip >/dev/null || die \"unzip not found in PATH\"\n        unzip -q \"$source\" -d \"$METADATA_WORK\"\n        root=\"$METADATA_WORK\"\n        ;;\n      *)\n        die \"metadata source must be a directory, .zip, or .tar.gz: $source\"\n        ;;\n    esac\n  else\n    die \"metadata source does not exist: $source\"\n  fi\n\n  metadata_root_from_dir \"$root\" ||\n    die \"metadata source does not contain a skills/ directory: $source\"\n}\n\nMETADATA_ROOT=\"$(prepare_metadata_root \"$METADATA_SOURCE\")\"\n\n# Pin tar.umask and extract with -p so staged modes are canonical 755/644\n# regardless of the builder's git config or process umask.\ngit -C \"$REPO_ROOT\" -c tar.umask=0022 archive --format=tar \"$REF\" -- \\\n  .codex-plugin \\\n  CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md \\\n  LICENSE \\\n  README.md \\\n  assets \\\n  skills \\\n  | tar -xpf - -C \"$STAGE\"\n\nVERSION=\"$(jq -r '.version // empty' \"$STAGE/.codex-plugin/plugin.json\")\"\n[[ -n \"$VERSION\" ]] || die \"could not read version from .codex-plugin/plugin.json\"\n\nif [[ -z \"$OUTPUT\" ]]; then\n  case \"$FORMAT\" in\n    zip)\n      OUTPUT=\"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers-$VERSION.zip\"\n      ;;\n    tar.gz)\n      OUTPUT=\"$REPO_ROOT/../_tmp/sup-codex-packaging/superpowers-$VERSION.tar.gz\"\n      ;;\n  esac\nfi\nmkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$OUTPUT\")\"\nOUTPUT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$OUTPUT\")\" && pwd)/$(basename \"$OUTPUT\")\"\n\nmissing_metadata=0\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n#\n# bump-version.sh — bump version numbers across all declared files,\n# with drift detection and repo-wide audit for missed files.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   bump-version.sh <new-version>   Bump all declared files to new version\n#   bump-version.sh --check         Report current versions (detect drift)\n#   bump-version.sh --audit         Check + grep repo for old version strings\n#\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/..\" && pwd)\"\nCONFIG=\"$REPO_ROOT/.version-bump.json\"\n\nif [[ ! -f \"$CONFIG\" ]]; then\n  echo \"error: .version-bump.json not found at $CONFIG\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\n# --- helpers ---\n\n# Read a dotted field path from a JSON file.\n# Handles both simple (\"version\") and nested (\"plugins.0.version\") paths.\nread_json_field() {\n  local file=\"$1\" field=\"$2\"\n  # Convert dot-path to jq path: \"plugins.0.version\" -> .plugins[0].version\n  local jq_path\n  jq_path=$(echo \"$field\" | sed -E 's/\\.([0-9]+)/[\\1]/g' | sed 's/^/./' | sed 's/\\.\\././g')\n  jq -r \"$jq_path\" \"$file\"\n}\n\n# Write a dotted field path in a JSON file, preserving formatting.\nwrite_json_field() {\n  local file=\"$1\" field=\"$2\" value=\"$3\"\n  local jq_path\n  jq_path=$(echo \"$field\" | sed -E 's/\\.([0-9]+)/[\\1]/g' | sed 's/^/./' | sed 's/\\.\\././g')\n  local tmp=\"${file}.tmp\"\n  jq \"$jq_path = \\\"$value\\\"\" \"$file\" > \"$tmp\" && mv \"$tmp\" \"$file\"\n}\n\n# Read the list of declared files from config.\n# Outputs lines of \"path<TAB>field\"\ndeclared_files() {\n  jq -r '.files[] | \"\\(.path)\\t\\(.field)\"' \"$CONFIG\"\n}\n\n# Read the audit exclude patterns from config.\naudit_excludes() {\n  jq -r '.audit.exclude[]' \"$CONFIG\" 2>/dev/null\n}\n\n# --- commands ---\n\ncmd_check() {\n  local has_drift=0\n  local versions=()\n\n  echo \"Version check:\"\n  echo \"\"\n\n  while IFS=$'\\t' read -r path field; do\n    local fullpath=\"$REPO_ROOT/$path\"\n    if [[ ! -f \"$fullpath\" ]]; then\n      printf \"  %-45s  MISSING\\n\" \"$path ($field)\"\n      has_drift=1\n      continue\n    fi\n    local ver\n    ver=$(read_json_field \"$fullpath\" \"$field\")\n    printf \"  %-45s  %s\\n\" \"$path ($field)\" \"$ver\"\n    versions+=(\"$ver\")\n  done < <(declared_files)\n\n  echo \"\"\n\n  # Check if all versions match\n  local unique\n  unique=$(printf '%s\\n' \"${versions[@]}\" | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')\n  if [[ \"$unique\" -gt 1 ]]; then\n    echo \"DRIFT DETECTED — versions are not in sync:\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"${versions[@]}\" | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn | while read -r count ver; do\n      echo \"  $ver ($count files)\"\n    done\n    has_drift=1\n  else\n    echo \"All declared files are in sync at ${versions[0]}\"\n  fi\n\n  return $has_drift\n}\n\ncmd_audit() {\n  # First run check\n  cmd_check || true\n  echo \"\"\n\n  # Determine the current version (most common across declared files)\n  local current_version\n  current_version=$(\n    while IFS=$'\\t' read -r path field; do\n      local fullpath=\"$REPO_ROOT/$path\"\n      [[ -f \"$fullpath\" ]] && read_json_field \"$fullpath\" \"$field\"\n    done < <(declared_files) | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn | head -1 | awk '{print $2}'\n  )\n\n  if [[ -z \"$current_version\" ]]; then\n    echo \"error: could not determine current version\" >&2\n    return 1\n  fi\n\n  echo \"Audit: scanning repo for version string '$current_version'...\"\n  echo \"\"\n\n  # Build grep exclude args\n  local -a exclude_args=()\n  while IFS= read -r pattern; do\n    exclude_args+=(\"--exclude=$pattern\" \"--exclude-dir=$pattern\")\n  done < <(audit_excludes)\n\n  # Also always exclude binary files and .git\n  exclude_args+=(\"--exclude-dir=.git\" \"--exclude-dir=node_modules\" \"--binary-files=without-match\")\n\n  # Get list of declared paths for comparison\n  local -a declared_paths=()\n  while IFS=$'\\t' read -r path _field; do\n    declared_paths+=(\"$path\")\n  done < <(declared_files)\n\n  # Grep for the version string\n  local found_undeclared=0\n  while IFS= read -r match; do\n    local match_file\n    match_file=$(echo \"$match\" | cut -d: -f1)\n    # Make path relative to repo root\n    local rel_path=\"${match_file#$REPO_ROOT/}\"\n\n    # Check if this file is in the declared list\n    local is_declared=0\n    for dp in \"${declared_paths[@]}\"; do\n      if [[ \"$rel_path\" == \"$dp\" ]]; then\n        is_declared=1\n        break\n      fi\n    done\n\n    if [[ \"$is_declared\" -eq 0 ]]; then\n      if [[ \"$found_undeclared\" -eq 0 ]]; then\n        echo \"UNDECLARED files containing '$current_version':\"\n        found_undeclared=1\n      fi\n      echo \"  $match\"\n    fi\n  done < <(grep -rn \"${exclude_args[@]}\" -F \"$current_version\" \"$REPO_ROOT\" 2>/dev/null || true)\n\n  if [[ \"$found_undeclared\" -eq 0 ]]; then\n    echo \"No undeclared files contain the version string. All clear.\"\n  else\n    echo \"\"\n    echo \"Review the above files — if they should be bumped, add them to .version-bump.json\"\n    echo \"If they should be skipped, add them to the audit.exclude list.\"\n  fi\n}\n\ncmd_bump() {\n  local new_version=\"$1\"\n\n  # Validate semver-ish format\n  if ! echo \"$new_version\" | grep -qE '^[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+'; then\n    echo \"error: '$new_version' doesn't look like a version (expected X.Y.Z)\" >&2\n    exit 1\n  fi\n\n  echo \"Bumping all declared files to $new_version...\"\n  echo \"\"\n\n  while IFS=$'\\t' read -r path field; do\n    local fullpath=\"$REPO_ROOT/$path\"\n    if [[ ! -f \"$fullpath\" ]]; then\n      echo \"  SKIP (missing): $path\"\n      continue\n    fi\n    local old_ver\n    old_ver=$(read_json_field \"$fullpath\" \"$field\")\n    write_json_field \"$fullpath\" \"$field\" \"$new_version\"\n    printf \"  %-45s  %s -> %s\\n\" \"$path ($field)\" \"$old_ver\" \"$new_version\"\n  done < <(declared_files)\n\n  echo \"\"\n  echo \"Done. Running audit to check for missed files...\"\n  echo \"\"\n  cmd_audit\n}\n\n# --- main ---\n\ncase \"${1:-}\" in\n  --check)\n    cmd_check\n    ;;\n  --audit)\n    cmd_audit\n    ;;\n  --help|-h|\"\")\n    echo \"Usage: bump-version.sh <new-version> | --check | --audit\"\n    echo \"\"\n    echo \"  <new-version>  Bump all declared files to the given version\"\n    echo \"  --check        Show current versions, detect drift\"\n    echo \"  --audit        Check + scan repo for undeclared version references\"\n    exit 0\n    ;;\n  --*)\n    echo \"error: unknown flag '$1'\" >&2\n    exit 1\n    ;;\n  *)\n    cmd_bump \"$1\"\n    ;;\nesac\n{\n  \"files\": [\n    { \"path\": \"package.json\", \"field\": \"version\" },\n    { \"path\": \".claude-plugin/plugin.json\", \"field\": \"version\" },\n    { \"path\": \".cursor-plugin/plugin.json\", \"field\": \"version\" },\n    { \"path\": \".codex-plugin/plugin.json\", \"field\": \"version\" },\n    { \"path\": \".kimi-plugin/plugin.json\", \"field\": \"version\" },\n    { \"path\": \".claude-plugin/marketplace.json\", \"field\": \"plugins.0.version\" },\n    { \"path\": \"gemini-extension.json\", \"field\": \"version\" }\n  ],\n  \"audit\": {\n    \"exclude\": [\n      \"CHANGELOG.md\",\n      \"RELEASE-NOTES.md\",\n      \"node_modules\",\n      \".git\",\n      \".version-bump.json\",\n      \"scripts/bump-version.sh\"\n    ]\n  }\n}\n"
  }
]

Repository files navigation\nL217: \nL218:   *   * cite101†README L219:   * cite101†Code of conduct L220:   * cite101†MIT license L221: \nL222: More items\nL223: \nL224: # Superpowers\nL225: \nL226: Superpowers is a complete software development methodology for your coding agents, built on top of a set of composable skills and some initial instructions that make sure your agent uses them.\nL227: ## Quickstart\nL228: \nL229: Give your agent Superpowers: cite102†Claude Code , cite103†Antigravity , cite104†Codex App , cite105†Codex CLI , cite106†Cursor , cite107†Factory Droid , cite108†Gemini CLI , cite109†GitHub Copilot CLI , cite110†Kimi Code , cite111†OpenCode , cite112†Pi .\nL230: ## How it works\nL231: \nL232: It starts from the moment you fire up your coding agent. As soon as it sees that you're building something, it doesn't just jump into trying to write code. Instead, it steps back and asks you what you're really trying to do.\nL233: \nL234: Once it's teased a spec out of the conversation, it shows it to you in chunks short enough to actually read and digest.\nL235: After you've signed off on the design, your agent puts together an implementation plan that's clear enough for an enthusiastic junior engineer with poor taste, no judgement, no project context, and an aversion to testing to follow. It emphasizes true red/green TDD, YAGNI (You Aren't Gonna Need It), and DRY.\nL236: Next up, once you say \"go\", it launches a subagent-driven-development process, having agents work through each engineering task, inspecting and reviewing their work, and continuing forward. It's not uncommon for your agent to work autonomously for a couple hours at a time without deviating from the plan you put together.\nL237: There's a bunch more to it, but that's the core of the system. And because the skills trigger automatically, you don't need to do anything special. Your coding agent just has Superpowers.\nL238: ## Commercial Services\nL239: \nL240: If you're using Superpowers in enterprise and could benefit from commercial support, additional tooling, or managed spending, please don't hesitate to drop us a line at sales@primeradiant.com.\nL241: \nL242: ## Installation\nL243: \nL244: Installation differs by harness. If you use more than one, install Superpowers separately for each one.\nL245: \nL246: ### Claude Code\nL247: \nL248: Superpowers is available via the cite113†official Claude plugin marketplace†claude.com L249: #### Official Marketplace\nL250: \nL251:   * Install the plugin from Anthropic's official marketplace:\nL252: \nL253:     /plugin install superpowers@claude-plugins-official\nL254: \nL255: #### Superpowers Marketplace\nL256: \nL257: The Superpowers marketplace provides Superpowers and some other related plugins for Claude Code.\nL258: \nL259:   * Register the marketplace:\nL260: \nL261:     /plugin marketplace add obra/superpowers-marketplace\nL262: \nL263:   * Install the plugin from this marketplace:\nL264: \nL265:     /plugin install superpowers@superpowers-marketplace\nL266: ### Antigravity\nL267: \nL268: Install Superpowers as a plugin from this repository:\nL269: \nL270:     agy plugin install https://github.com/obra/superpowers\nL271: \nL272: Antigravity runs the plugin's session-start hook, so Superpowers is active from the first message. Reinstall with the same command to update.\nL273: ### Codex App\nL274: \nL275: Superpowers is available via the cite114†official Codex plugin marketplace .\nL276: \nL277:   * In the Codex app, click on Plugins in the sidebar.\nL278:   * You should see `Superpowers` in the Coding section.\nL279:   * Click the `+` next to Superpowers and follow the prompts.\nL280: \nL281: ### Codex CLI\nL282: \nL283: Superpowers is available via the cite114†official Codex plugin marketplace .\nL284: \nL285:   * Open the plugin search interface:\nL286: \nL287:     /plugins\nL288: \nL289:   * Search for Superpowers:\nL290: \nL291:     superpowers\nL292: \nL293:   * Select `Install Plugin`.\nL294: ### Cursor\nL295: \nL296:   * In Cursor Agent chat, install from marketplace:\nL297: \nL298:     `/add-plugin superpowers\nL299:     `\nL300: \nL301:   * Or search for \"superpowers\" in the plugin marketplace.\nL302: \nL303: ### Factory Droid\nL304: \nL305:   * Register the marketplace:\nL306: \nL307:     droid plugin marketplace add https://github.com/obra/superpowers\nL308: \nL309:   * Install the plugin:\nL310: \nL311:     droid plugin install superpowers@superpowers\nL312: ### Gemini CLI\nL313: \nL314:   * Install the extension:\nL315: \nL316:     gemini extensions install https://github.com/obra/superpowers\nL317: \nL318:   * Update later:\nL319: \nL320:     gemini extensions update superpowers\nL321: \nL322: ### GitHub Copilot CLI\nL323: \nL324:   * Register the marketplace:\nL325: \nL326:     copilot plugin marketplace add obra/superpowers-marketplace\nL327: \nL328:   * Install the plugin:\nL329: \nL330:     copilot plugin install superpowers@superpowers-marketplace\nL331: ### Kimi Code\nL332: \nL333: Superpowers is available in Kimi Code's plugin marketplace.\nL334: \nL335:   * Open Kimi Code's plugin manager:\nL336: \nL337:     `/plugins\nL338:     `\nL339: \nL340:   * Go to `Marketplace` > `Superpowers` and install it.\nL341: \nL342:   * Or install directly from this repository:\nL343: \nL344:     `/plugins install https://github.com/obra/superpowers\nL345:     `\nL346: \nL347:   * Detailed docs: cite115†docs/README.kimi.md L348: ### OpenCode\nL349: \nL350: OpenCode uses its own plugin install; install Superpowers separately even if you already use it in another harness.\nL351: \nL352:   * Tell OpenCode:\nL353: \nL354:     `Fetch and follow instructions from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/obra/superpowers/refs/heads/main/.opencode/INSTALL.md\nL355:     `\nL356: \nL357:   * Detailed docs: cite116†docs/README.opencode.md L358: ### Pi\nL359: \nL360: Install Superpowers as a Pi package from this repository:\nL361: \nL362:     pi install git:github.com/obra/superpowers\nL363: \nL364: For local development, run Pi with this checkout loaded as a temporary package:\nL365: \nL366:     pi -e /path/to/superpowers\nL367: The Pi package loads the Superpowers skills and a small extension that injects the `using-superpowers` bootstrap at session startup and again after compaction. Pi has native skills, so no compatibility `Skill` tool is required. Subagent and task-list tools remain optional Pi companion packages.\nL368: ## The Basic Workflow\nL369: \nL370:   1. brainstorming - Activates before writing code. Refines rough ideas through questions, explores alternatives, presents design in sections for validation. Saves design document.\nL371: \nL372:   2. using-git-worktrees - Activates after design approval. Creates isolated workspace on new branch, runs project setup, verifies clean test baseline.\nL373:   3. writing-plans - Activates with approved design. Breaks work into bite-sized tasks (2-5 minutes each). Every task has exact file paths, complete code, verification steps.\nL374: \nL375:   4. subagent-driven-development or executing-plans - Activates with plan. Dispatches fresh subagent per task with two-stage review (spec compliance, then code quality), or executes in batches with human checkpoints.\nL376:   5. test-driven-development - Activates during implementation. Enforces RED-GREEN-REFACTOR: write failing test, watch it fail, write minimal code, watch it pass, commit. Deletes code written before tests.\nL377: \nL378:   6. requesting-code-review - Activates between tasks. Reviews against plan, reports issues by severity. Critical issues block progress.\nL379: \nL380:   7. finishing-a-development-branch - Activates when tasks complete. Verifies tests, presents options (merge/PR/keep/discard), cleans up worktree.\nL381: The agent checks for relevant skills before any task. Mandatory workflows, not suggestions.\nL382: ## What's Inside\nL383: ### Skills Library\nL384: \nL385: Testing\nL386: \nL387:   * test-driven-development - RED-GREEN-REFACTOR cycle (includes testing anti-patterns reference)\nL388: \nL389: Debugging\nL390: \nL391:   * systematic-debugging - 4-phase root cause process (includes root-cause-tracing, defense-in-depth, condition-based-waiting techniques)\nL392:   * verification-before-completion - Ensure it's actually fixed\nL393: \nL394: Collaboration\nL395:   * brainstorming - Socratic design refinement\nL396:   * writing-plans - Detailed implementation plans\nL397:   * executing-plans - Batch execution with checkpoints\nL398:   * dispatching-parallel-agents - Concurrent subagent workflows\nL399:   * requesting-code-review - Pre-review checklist\nL400:   * receiving-code-review - Responding to feedback\nL401:   * using-git-worktrees - Parallel development branches\nL402:   * finishing-a-development-branch - Merge/PR decision workflow\nL403:   * subagent-driven-development - Fast iteration with two-stage review (spec compliance, then code quality)\nL404: Meta\nL405: \nL406:   * writing-skills - Create new skills following best practices (includes testing methodology)\nL407:   * using-superpowers - Introduction to the skills system\nL408: ## Philosophy\nL409: \nL410:   * Test-Driven Development - Write tests first, always\nL411:   * Systematic over ad-hoc - Process over guessing\nL412:   * Complexity reduction - Simplicity as primary goal\nL413:   * Evidence over claims - Verify before declaring success\nL414: \nL415: Read cite117†the original release announcement†blog.fsck.com .\nL416: ## Contributing\nL417: \nL418: The general contribution process for Superpowers is below. Keep in mind that we don't generally accept contributions of new skills and that any updates to skills must work across all of the coding agents we support.\nL419: \nL420:   1. Fork the repository\nL421:   2. Switch to the 'dev' branch\nL422:   3. Create a branch for your work\nL423:   4. Follow the `writing-skills` skill for creating and testing new and modified skills\nL424:   5. Submit a PR, being sure to fill in the pull request template.\nL425: Skill-behavior tests use the drill eval harness from cite118†superpowers-evals , cloned into `evals/` — see `evals/README.md` for setup. Plugin-infrastructure tests live at `tests/` and run via the relevant `run-*.sh` or `npm test`.\nL426: \nL427: See `skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md` for the complete guide.\nL428: ## Updating\nL429: \nL430: Superpowers updates are somewhat coding-agent dependent, but are often automatic.\nL431: \nL432: ## License\nL433: \nL434: MIT License - see LICENSE file for details\nL435: ## Visual companion telemetry\nL436: Because skills and plugins don't provide any feedback to creators, we have no idea how many of you are using Superpowers. By default, the Prime Radiant logo on brainstorming's optional visual companion feature is loaded from our website. It includes the version of Superpowers in use. It does not include any details about your project, prompt, or coding agent. We don't see your clicks or anything about what you're building.\nL437: This helps us have a rough idea of how many folks are using Superpowers and which version of Superpowers they're using. It's 100% optional. To disable this, set the environment variable `SUPERPOWERS_DISABLE_TELEMETRY` to any true value. Superpowers also honors Claude Code's `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC` opt-outs.\nL438: ## Community\nL439: \nL440: Superpowers is built by cite119†Jesse Vincent†blog.fsck.com and the rest of the folks at cite120†Prime Radiant†primeradiant.com .\nL441: \nL442:   * Discord: cite121†Join us†discord.gg for community support, questions, and sharing what you're building with Superpowers\nL443:   * Issues: cite66†https://github.com/obra/superpowers/issues L444:   * Release announcements: cite122†Sign up†primeradiant.com to get notified about ````json
[
  {

  {
    "type": "input_text",
    "text": "{\"content\":\"# Superpowers Evals\\n\\nBehavioral eval lab for [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers).\\n**Quorum** drives real coding-agent CLIs (Claude, Codex, Antigravity, Gemini,\\nHermes, Kimi, OpenCode, Pi, and Copilot) through a Gauntlet QA agent and grades them\\nagainst scenario acceptance criteria plus deterministic post-checks.\\n\\nCode, CLI, paths, and inline prose all use lowercase `quorum`; the capitalized\\nform `Quorum` appears in headings and the actor table.\\n\\nThis is not a generic benchmark suite. It is an eval lab for workflow\\ncompliance: skill triggering, worktree behavior, subagent coordination,\\nverification reflexes, review quality, and cost-shaping patterns.\\n\\n## Safety Model\\n\\nquorum has two very different execution modes:\\n\\n- **Static/unit checks** are safe for public CI. They run `biome`, `tsc`, and\\n  `bun test`. They do not call model APIs and do not launch agent CLIs.\\n- **Live evals** are trusted-maintainer operations. They launch Claude Code,\\n  Codex CLI, Antigravity CLI, Gemini CLI, Hermes CLI, Kimi Code, OpenCode CLI, Pi CLI, or\\n  Copilot CLI in permissive modes and collect raw transcripts, tool calls,\\n  filesystem state, and session logs.\\n\\nPublic CI must stay on the static/unit side of that line. Never add API keys,\\nlive `quorum run …` invocations, or dangerous-mode agent launches to public\\nCI.\\n\\n## Live Eval Risk\\n\\nLive evals run the Coding-Agent under test with broad execution power:\\n\\n- Claude uses `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.\\n- Codex uses `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`.\\n- Antigravity uses `--dangerously-skip-permissions` and relies on local\\n  browser/keyring auth for `agy`.\\n- Gemini uses `--skip-trust --approval-mode=yolo`; API-key auth is default,\\n  with opt-in OAuth auth for trusted local runs.\\n- Hermes uses `--yolo` and API-key auth in the run-local .env.\\n- Kimi uses `--yolo`.\\n- OpenCode uses `--dangerously-skip-permissions`.\\n- Pi uses explicit tool allowlists and API-key auth in a run-local config dir.\\n- Copilot uses `--allow-all`.\\n\\nquorum pins each Coding-Agent's `HOME` (plus the XDG base dirs and `TMPDIR`)\\nto a throwaway per-run home at `<run>/home` — the launcher splices in the\\n`$QUORUM_HOME_ENV` token built by `src/agents/home-env.ts` (`xdgHomeEnv`, the\\nsingle source of truth). Each agent's config dir is collapsed *under* that home\\n(Claude `.claude`, Codex `.codex`, Gemini `.`, Hermes `.hermes`, OpenCode `.`, Antigravity `.`,\\nCopilot `.copilot`, Kimi `.kimi-code`, Pi `.pi/agent`), so the Coding-Agent\\nfinds its config via its own `$HOME` default and never sees the host's real\\n`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.kimi-code`, `~/.pi`, `~/.copilot`,\\n`~/.config`, or other home-relative state, installed plugins, or prior sessions.\\nProvisioning seeds the config — and the host OAuth creds each agent needs — into\\nthat throwaway home before launch, so there is no run-time login. Copilot also\\nstages the local Superpowers plugin under the isolated home, uses an\\nallowlisted outer environment, and writes a secret-bearing chmod-0600\\n`.copilot-env` inside the run dir. That narrows the blast radius but is not a\\nsandbox. OpenCode and Copilot launchers additionally use allowlisted\\nenvironments, but live Coding-Agents still run with broad filesystem and\\ncommand execution power.\\n\\nRun live evals only from a trusted local environment:\\n\\n- Export only the API key needed for the selected Coding-Agent.\\n- Avoid running with broad production or personal secrets in the environment.\\n- Treat `results/`, raw session logs, session-state/tool-call artifacts, and\\n  Gauntlet-Agent inputs as\\n  sensitive.\\n- Do not commit or paste raw run artifacts without checking them first.\\n\\n## Quick Start\\n\\nInstall and run the static gates:\\n\\n```bash\\nbun install\\nbun run check\\nbun run quorum check\\n```\\n\\nRun one local or break-glass scenario outside the container:\\n\\n```bash\\nexport SUPERPOWERS_ROOT=/path/to/superpowers\\nexport ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/triggering-writing-plans --coding-agent claude\\nbun run quorum show <run-dir>\\n```\\n\\nThe Gauntlet-Agent (QA driver) authenticates to Anthropic with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`\\nby default. To drive it from a logged-in Claude subscription instead, set\\n`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (from `claude setup-token`) in the environment (e.g.\\n`.env`); the harness passes it through and gauntlet prefers it over the API key.\\nNote: a subscription has usage caps sized for interactive use — high-concurrency\\n`run-all` batches can hit them, so the API key remains the better fit for heavy load.\\n\\nAgent names are `claude`, `codex`, `antigravity`, `gemini`, `kimi`,\\n`opencode`, `pi`, and `copilot`. Not every scenario is valid for every agent.\\n\\n**BREAKING (credential axis)**: `claude-haiku` and `claude-sonnet` are no\\nlonger separate agent names. To run the Claude harness against Sonnet or Haiku:\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent claude --credential sonnet\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent claude --credential haiku\\n```\\n\\nThe `claude` agent's default credential is `opus`.\\n\\n## Shared Eval Appliance\\n\\nShared remote live evals are designed to run from a trusted appliance host with\\none blessed credential bundle, exact repo/ref provenance, host locks, and\\nrecoverable job records. Agents should use the appliance helper once it exists\\non the configured host:\\n\\n```bash\\nevals-appliance doctor --json\\nevals-appliance prepare --json --superpowers-ref <branch-tag-or-sha>\\nevals-appliance run-all --json --detach \\\\\\n  --superpowers-ref <branch-tag-or-sha> \\\\\\n  -- --tier sentinel \\\\\\n     --coding-agents claude,codex,kimi \\\\\\n     --jobs 4\\nevals-appliance status --json <job-id>\\nevals-appliance show --json <job-id>\\nevals-appliance costs --json <job-id>\\nevals-appliance cancel --json <job-id>\\n```\\n\\nThe target interface and operating rules are in\\n[docs/appliance-runbook.md](docs/appliance-runbook.md), backed by\\n[docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-18-shared-eval-appliance-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-18-shared-eval-appliance-design.md).\\n`doctor` is read-only. `prepare` returns `lock_busy` rather than changing refs\\nwhile a live job is active.\\nHost access and provider-specific break-glass procedures are intentionally kept\\nout of this public repo; use the private ops runbook for those details.\\nRaw `bun run quorum ...` and `scripts/evals-container exec quorum ...` remain\\nlocal or trusted break-glass workflows for shared live evals.\\n\\n## Container Runtime\\n\\nThe Docker runtime is the primary recipe for real suite runs. It keeps the\\nevals checkout, the Superpowers checkout under test, credentials, auth sources,\\nand all run artifacts on the host while quorum runs inside a rich Ubuntu\\nworkspace container.\\n\\nCreate `.env.container` or pass an explicit env file to `up`:\\n\\n```dotenv\\nANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...\\nOPENAI_API_KEY=...\\nOPENROUTER_API_KEY=...      # Pi default: OpenRouter GLM 5.2\\nGEMINI_API_KEY=...          # or GEMINI_AUTH_TYPE=oauth-personal\\nKIMI_MODEL_API_KEY=...      # unless using mounted Kimi OAuth\\nPI_PROVIDER=...             # only for raw/custom Pi env auth outside the default credential\\nPI_MODEL=...\\nPI_API_KEY=...\\nCOPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN=...\\n```\\n\\nThen build, start, and validate the container:\\n\\n```bash\\nscripts/evals-container build\\nscripts/evals-container down || true\\nscripts/evals-container --env-file .env.container up\\nscripts/evals-container exec evals-tool-versions\\nscripts/evals-container exec quorum check\\n```\\n\\nThe wrapper mounts this evals checkout at `/workspace/evals`, the parent\\nSuperpowers checkout at `/workspace/superpowers`, and host `results/` at\\n`/workspace/evals/results`. Override the Superpowers checkout with\\n`--superpowers-root <dir>` when the default parent path is not the system under\\ntest.\\n\\nThe image build needs a local Gauntlet checkout. The wrapper discovers it\\nfrom `GAUNTLET_ROOT` or a Bun global `bun link` install; use\\n`--gauntlet-root <dir>` with `build` to choose explicitly.\\n\\nCredentials are read-only mounts. By default, `up` uses `.env.container` first,\\nthen `.env`, and mounts the first one found at `/run/evals/credentials.env`.\\nPass `--env-file <file>` before `up` to choose explicitly. The wrapper does not\\npass the host environment wholesale; only the in-container `quorum` shim sources\\nthe dotenv file, so `scripts/evals-container exec bash ...` does not\\nautomatically receive live eval credentials. Use `down` before changing the\\nenv-file mount on an existing container.\\n\\nOAuth/file auth sources are also read-only. Existing `~/.codex`, `~/.gemini`,\\n`~/.kimi-code`, and `~/.pi` directories mount to `/auth/codex`, `/auth/gemini`,\\n`/auth/kimi-code`, and `/auth/pi`. Use `--auth codex=<dir>`,\\n`--auth gemini=<dir>`, `--auth kimi=<dir>`, or `--auth pi=<dir>` to override a\\nsource.\\n\\nStart with the sentinel suite:\\n\\n```bash\\nscripts/evals-container exec quorum run-all \\\\\\n  --tier sentinel \\\\\\n  --coding-agents claude,codex,kimi \\\\\\n  --jobs 4\\n\\nfor agent in gemini opencode pi copilot; do\\n  scripts/evals-container exec quorum run-all \\\\\\n    --tier sentinel \\\\\\n    --coding-agents \\\"$agent\\\" \\\\\\n    --jobs 1\\ndone\\n```\\n\\nRun the same commands without `--tier sentinel` for the full ready suite.\\n`run-all` writes each batch under `results/batches/<batch-id>/` and each run\\nunder `results/<scenario>-<agent>-<os>-<timestamp>-<nonce>/`; render a batch\\nwith:\\n\\n```bash\\nscripts/evals-container exec quorum show <batch-id>\\n```\\n\\n`run-all` prints a periodic liveness heartbeat\\n(`⋯ … · running N/jobs · done D · queued Q · [agent:scenario, …]`); tune it with\\n`--heartbeat-seconds <n>` (`0` disables). Interrupting a batch — Ctrl-C, or the\\n`exec` session closing — stops it gracefully: the queue is cancelled, in-flight\\nruns are SIGINT'd (and recorded as stopped), and the batch footer is still\\nwritten, so `finished_at` is never left null.\\n\\nThe container runtime does not mount the Docker socket, publish dashboard ports,\\nor include desktop IDEs. The image omits Antigravity's desktop `agy` installer;\\nrun Antigravity host-side until there is a headless install path:\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run quorum run-all --coding-agents antigravity --jobs 1\\n```\\n\\nFor grouped all-agent host sweeps, per-agent credentials, auth mount details,\\nand troubleshooting, use [docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md](docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md).\\n\\n## Windows Runtime\\n\\nFor evals on Windows 11, use `--os windows` (Linux+KVM hosts only):\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent claude --os windows\\n```\\n\\nSee [docs/windows/eval-runtime.md](docs/windows/eval-runtime.md) for setup and deployment.\\n\\n## Canonical Actors\\n\\nKeep the actors straight; confusing them is the most common triage error.\\nThese names are used everywhere — docs, CLI output, code, filenames, commit\\nmessages.\\n\\n| Actor | What it is | Where it lives / its files |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| **Gauntlet** | General-purpose QA framework; the `gauntlet` CLI. A black-box tester. | repo `github.com/prime-radiant-inc/gauntlet`; on `PATH` as `gauntlet` (via `bun link` or `GAUNTLET_ROOT`) |\\n| **Gauntlet-Agent** | The LLM *inside* Gauntlet that drives the Coding-Agent and self-grades against the story's ACs. | model e.g. `claude-sonnet-4-6`; event stream → `<run>/gauntlet-agent/results/<runId>/run.jsonl`; verdict → `result.{json,md}` |\\n| **Coding-Agent** | The agent under test — the SUT. Instances: **Claude**, **Codex**, **Antigravity**, **Gemini**, **Kimi**, **OpenCode**, **Pi**, **Copilot**. | config + session log under its throwaway `$HOME` at `<run>/home/…`; the files it writes → `<run>/coding-agent-workdir/` |\\n| **Quorum** | The TypeScript/Bun wrapper. Owns setup, Coding-Agent adaptation, deterministic checks, and the final verdict. | repo `superpowers-evals/src/`; `<run>/verdict.json` |\\n\\nA run involves **two** LLMs — the **Gauntlet-Agent** (QA tester) and the\\n**Coding-Agent** (subject). Separate models, separate logs, separate token\\ncosts.\\n\\n## Operator Guides\\n\\n- [docs/scenario-authoring.md](docs/scenario-authoring.md) - scenario anatomy,\\n  story/AC craft, setup helpers, check verbs, and authoring traps.\\n- [docs/appliance-runbook.md](docs/appliance-runbook.md) - shared remote\\n  appliance operating rules for agents.\\n- [docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md](docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md)\\n  - credentials, sweeps, per-agent runtime notes, and troubleshooting.\\n- [docs/adding-a-coding-agent.md](docs/adding-a-coding-agent.md) - checklist\\n  for adding a new agent target, launcher, provisioner, normalizer, and smoke.\\n- [docs/superpowers/skills/triaging-a-failing-eval.md](docs/superpowers/skills/triaging-a-failing-eval.md)\\n  - attribution atlas for non-passing runs.\\n- [docs/baselines/](docs/baselines/) - current known-good baselines by backend.\\n\\n## Credential Axis\\n\\nThe eval dimension is **(scenario, coding-agent, credential, os)**. `credentials.yaml`\\nat the repo root defines named credentials; each entry declares the model, wire\\nprotocol (`api`: `openai-chat`, `openai-responses`, `anthropic`, or `gemini`),\\noptional `base_url` for non-default endpoints, auth type (`api-key`,\\n`subscription`, or `oauth`), optional `api_key_env`, the runtime families it\\nserves (`harnesses`), and optional scheduler overrides (`max_concurrency`,\\n`launch_spacing_seconds`) and a `compat` block (`thinking_format`,\\n`max_tokens_field`).\\n\\nEach agent YAML declares a `default_credential`. Override at runtime:\\n\\n```bash\\n# run against a named credential\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent claude --credential sonnet\\n\\n# run-all against multiple credentials (incompatible cells are skipped)\\nbun run quorum run-all --coding-agents claude,opencode --credentials sonnet,haiku,opencode_gpt5 --jobs 4\\n```\\n\\n`quorum check` validates `credentials.yaml` and each agent's `default_credential`.\\n\\nThe scheduler keys its concurrency cap and rate-limit latch on the credential's\\n**limiterKey** — the credential's `base_url` if set, else the credential name,\\njoined with its `api` (e.g. `https://…/v1|openai-chat`, or `opus|anthropic` for\\na native credential with no `base_url`). Cells sharing a limiterKey share one\\ncap and one rate-limit latch: a rate-limit response on any cell immediately\\nskips all remaining queued cells for that endpoint.\\n\\nStandard named credentials (see `credentials.yaml`): `opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`\\n(Claude harness), `codex_sub` (Codex subscription), `kimi_default`,\\n`openrouter_glm_5_2` (Pi default), `pi_default` (native Pi OAuth opt-in),\\n`opencode_gpt5`, `gemini_default`, `serf_default`, `glm_5_2_chat`,\\n`glm_5_2_responses`, `ollama_local`.\\n\\n### External Serf campaigns\\n\\nShort-lived Serf model/provider campaigns use an external credentials file,\\nnot the repository's canonical `credentials.yaml`. Pass it explicitly with\\n`--credentials-file` to `quorum run`, `quorum run-all`, or `quorum check`.\\nKeep the real campaign YAML and all raw run artifacts outside Git: the YAML\\ncontains routing labels and the selected API-key environment-variable name,\\nnever a key value.\\n\\nEach campaign preset must pin exactly one model and one provider, disable\\nfallbacks, and contain no prompt, sampling, reasoning, tool, or token-limit\\noverrides. Supply its dedicated key through the trusted runtime credential\\nbundle. The key must enforce the campaign's intended data policy, have a\\ncampaign spend cap, and, for a shared-capacity campaign, have no BYOK binding.\\nA BYOK comparison is a separate campaign with a separate key and candidate\\nfile.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, `run-all` parses the external file once and writes its\\ncanonical snapshot to\\n`results/batches/<batch-id>/credentials.snapshot.yaml`; every child receives\\nthat immutable snapshot. A direct `quorum run` writes the same canonical\\nsnapshot under its run directory. Editing the source YAML after a batch starts\\ncannot change later cells. Snapshots contain schema-known routing metadata and\\nenvironment-variable names, not secret values, but they remain part of the\\nsensitive run artifacts.\\n\\nRun the agent-neutral smoke first, then run the expensive scenario only for\\ncredentials whose final smoke verdict is `pass`:\\n\\n```bash\\nquorum run-all \\\\\\n  --scenarios 00-quorum-smoke-hello-world \\\\\\n  --include-drafts \\\\\\n  --coding-agents serf \\\\\\n  --credentials-file /secure/campaign.yaml \\\\\\n  --credentials serf_example_a \\\\\\n  --jobs 1\\n\\nquorum run-all \\\\\\n  --scenarios serf-builder-fractals \\\\\\n  --coding-agents serf \\\\\\n  --credentials-file /secure/campaign.yaml \\\\\\n  --credentials serf_example_a \\\\\\n  --jobs 1\\n```\\n\\n`--jobs 1` is the sequential latency/cost baseline. One matrix cell is one\\npaid attempt; the campaign scheduler does not automatically retry or repeat a\\ncell. Render the labeled comparison with `quorum costs <batch-id>`. Only final\\n`pass` rows are marked comparable; `fail` and `indeterminate` remain visible\\nbut unranked, and missing measurements render as missing rather than zero.\\nCharged, estimated, and delta columns are Coding-Agent costs. The existing\\n`--with-gauntlet` columns are separate Gauntlet-Agent harness overhead.\\n\\nLive acceptance is manual trusted-maintainer work, never public CI automation:\\n\\n1. Run one known-good hello-world cell with `--jobs 1`.\\n2. Inspect `verdict.json`, `trajectory.json`,\\n   `openrouter-generations.json`, `coding-agent-token-usage.json`, and\\n   `quorum costs <batch-id>`. Confirm model, provider, preset version, BYOK is\\n   false, token/cache buckets, duration, charged cost, estimate, delta, and\\n   candidate labels, including quantization and catalog date.\\n3. Run one Fractals cell with `--jobs 1`; require final `pass`, every\\n   deterministic check, committed main-checkout delivery, and a complete\\n   comparison row.\\n4. Run two hello-world candidates with `--jobs 2`; confirm distinct\\n   attribution with no cross-contaminated keys, generations, labels, or\\n   economics.\\n5. Only then run one sequential Fractals cell per smoke-passing candidate.\\n\\nPublish only release-reviewed, sanitized conclusions in a dated\\n`docs/experiments/` note, recording failures as well as wins. External campaign\\nYAML and raw artifacts stay outside Git.\\n\\n## Core Commands\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run quorum list\\nbun run quorum new my-new-scenario\\nbun run quorum check my-new-scenario\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent <agent>\\nbun run quorum run scenarios/<name> --coding-agent claude --credential sonnet\\nbun run quorum run-all --coding-agents claude,codex --jobs 2\\nbun run quorum run-all --coding-agents claude --credentials sonnet,haiku --jobs 2\\nbun run quorum show <run-or-batch-id>\\nbun run quorum costs <run-or-batch-id>\\n```\\n\\n`quorum check` with no arguments validates every scenario and `credentials.yaml`.\\n`run-all` runs every included scenario against every selected Coding-Agent,\\nfiltered by each scenario's `# coding-agents:` directive.\\n\\n## Verdicts And Artifacts\\n\\nquorum produces a three-valued verdict:\\n\\n- `pass` - Gauntlet-Agent passed and every post-check passed.\\n- `fail` - Gauntlet-Agent failed, or a post-check failed.\\n- `indeterminate` - setup/pre-check/capture/quorum failure, Gauntlet\\n  `investigate`, or empty trace when trace checks are present.\\n\\nExit codes are 0 for `pass`, 1 for `fail`, and 2 for `indeterminate`.\\n\\nEach run produces one directory under `results/`:\\n\\n```text\\nresults/<scenario>-<coding-agent>-<os>-<timestamp>-<nonce>/\\n|-- verdict.json                     composed result; start here\\n|-- gauntlet-agent/                  Gauntlet-Agent evidence\\n|-- coding-agent-workdir/            files the Coding-Agent produced\\n|-- home/                            throwaway Coding-Agent HOME\\n|-- trajectory.json                  normalized ATIF trace\\n`-- coding-agent-token-usage.json    Coding-Agent token cost, when priced\\n```\\n\\n`results/` is gitignored because run artifacts can contain sensitive\\ntranscripts, credentials, tool calls, and filesystem state.\\n\\n## Safe Checks\\n\\nThese are the checks expected in CI and on routine PRs:\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run check          # biome ci . && tsc --noEmit && bun test — the full gate\\nbun run quorum check   # validate every scenario directory\\n```\\n\\n`bun run check` is the single gate (Biome lint/format + full-strict `tsc` +\\n`bun test`); individual steps are `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, and\\n`bun test`.\\n\\n## Architecture\\n\\nquorum is **TypeScript on Bun**. The console is `bun run quorum <cmd>` (a\\n[commander](https://github.com/tj/commander.js) CLI at `src/cli/index.ts`, also\\nexposed as the `quorum` bin); the gate is `bun run check`\\n(Biome + full-strict `tsc` + `bun test`).\\n\\nThe shapes that cross process and file boundaries — `verdict.json`, batch\\nindices, economics, the Gauntlet result, agent YAML — are **zod schemas** in\\n`src/contracts/`, validated at every boundary, so a malformed external file\\nfails loudly instead of corrupting a verdict. The `cli/` layer parses commands\\nand dispatches into the `runner/` pipeline (one scenario × one Coding-Agent) or\\n`run-all/` (the matrix). Per-Coding-Agent differences live in two parallel\\nfan-outs keyed by agent name: `agents/` seeds the agent's config under the\\nthrowaway per-run `$HOME` (`<run>/home`), and `normalize/` turns that agent's\\nsession log into a uniform tool-call trace. Live agent-CLI calls and other\\nnon-hermetic subprocesses go through the\\n`agents/command-runner.ts` seam, so the unit suite injects fakes and never\\nlaunches a real CLI. `scheduler/` is the shared concurrency engine under\\n`run-all/`. The dashboard is a separate read-only package that scans `results/`\\nand `grid-manifest.json`. `env.ts` is the only module that reads `process.env`.\\n\\n```text\\nsrc/\\n  cli/                  commander CLI: run, list, new, check, show, costs, run-all, grid-manifest\\n    index.ts              command wiring + run / costs / run-all / grid-manifest actions\\n    render.ts             verdict renderer for triage (quorum show)\\n    render-batch.ts       batch-matrix renderer (quorum show <batch>)\\n    resolve-target.ts     run/batch target resolution; scenario.ts scenario loading\\n  runner/               per-run orchestration (one scenario × one Coding-Agent)\\n    index.ts              setup → pre-checks → gauntlet drive → capture → post-checks → compose\\n    context.ts            populate the Gauntlet-Agent context dir (HOWTO + launch-agent shim)\\n    phase.ts              phase.json (setup/agent/checks) for the dashboard\\n    stopped.ts            SIGINT → stopped (indeterminate) verdict; errors.ts staged run-error stages\\n  agents/               per-Coding-Agent provisioning (resolveAgent dispatch)\\n    index.ts              agent registry + dispatch (incl. the inline Claude/Default adapters)\\n    command-runner.ts     injectable subprocess seam (live CLIs faked in tests)\\n    <agent>.ts            codex/gemini/kimi/opencode/pi/copilot/antigravity adapters\\n  normalize/            session-log → normalized tool-call trace, one module per dialect\\n  capture/              session-log snapshot/diff + tool-call capture + token usage; cwd-filter\\n  obol/                 obol cost estimation (session-log + gauntlet sidecar)\\n  economics.ts          token-cost composition → coding-agent-token-usage.json\\n  composer.ts           three-valued verdict from the gauntlet + checks layers\\n  checks/               sources prelude.sh + checks.sh, runs pre()/post(), collects check records\\n    prelude.sh            bare-verb DSL: defines each check verb as a bash function that\\n                          delegates to the TS dispatchers (no bin/ shims, no PATH prepend)\\n  scheduler/            central concurrency dispatcher (one global slot pool, per-harness limits + spacing)\\n  run-all/              scenario × Coding-Agent matrix over the scheduler; batch index\\n  setup-helpers/        scenario fixture builders + the `setup-helpers` CLI (dispatch registry)\\n  contracts/            zod schemas at the JSON boundaries (verdict, batch, economics, gauntlet, agent-config)\\n  scaffold.ts           `quorum new` / `quorum check`\\n  setup-step.ts         runs scenario setup.sh (sources prelude.sh via BASH_ENV so bare verbs resolve)\\n  story-meta.ts         story.md frontmatter (quorum_max_time, quorum_tier, status)\\n  env.ts                the single process.env boundary\\n  paths.ts              repo root, UTC stamps, nonces\\n  invariant.ts          assertNever exhaustiveness guard for closed unions\\n  check/                typed check verbs: fs-verbs.ts (file/git/env + bootstrap),\\n                        dispatch.ts (table + `not`), transcript-dispatch.ts, record.ts (sole emitter)\\n  cli/check-tool.ts     the dispatcher behind every check verb function (file-exists,\\n                        file-contains, command-succeeds, git-*, assert-checkout-clean,\\n                        requires-tool, not, files-exist, the *-installed/hook/extension\\n                        checks); check-transcript.ts and setup-helpers/cli.ts are the\\n                        other two dispatchers the prelude delegates to\\n  cli/list-check-verbs.ts  prints the FS_VERBS verb set the prelude loops over (drift-proof)\\ncoding-agents/          per-Coding-Agent material:\\n  <name>.yaml             CLI config\\n  <name>-context/         HOWTO prose and launchers for the Gauntlet-Agent\\nscenarios/              scenarios (one directory each)\\nfixtures/               shared static fixture repos (e.g. template-repo/, sdd-*/)\\ntest/                   bun test suite\\ndocs/                   design notes, specs, plans, testing protocols, baselines\\npackages/dashboard/     read-only web matrix UI: scan/view, typed HTML templates, SSE bus, Bun.serve\\n```\\n\\n## Triage\\n\\nTriaging a non-passing run starts with:\\n\\n```bash\\nbun run quorum show [<target>]\\n```\\n\\nThen use [docs/superpowers/skills/triaging-a-failing-eval.md](docs/superpowers/skills/triaging-a-failing-eval.md)\\nfor the attribution atlas. For agent-specific auth, provisioning, and capture\\nchecks, use [docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md](docs/coding-agent-care-and-feeding.md).\\n\\nFor the current known-good baseline, see [docs/baselines/](docs/baselines/).\\n\\n## Contribution Rules\\n\\nThis repo inherits the quality bar of `superpowers`.\\n\\n- One problem per PR.\\n- Do not commit generated run artifacts or secrets.\\n- Do not add live evals to public CI.\\n- Use the PR template and explain the security/eval-lab risk for changes that\\n  touch Coding-Agent configs, shell execution, setup helpers, check tools, or\\n  Gauntlet-Agent input.\\n- Changes to behavior-shaping eval methodology need evidence, not just prose.\\n\\n## Parent Submodule Bump\\n\\n`superpowers-evals` is consumed by `superpowers` as the `evals` submodule.\\nAfter any PR merges to `main` here, open a follow-up PR against the parent\\n`superpowers` repo targeting `dev` that bumps the `evals` submodule pointer to\\nthe merged `superpowers-evals` commit.\\n\\nDo not treat a `superpowers-evals` merge as fully propagated until that parent\\nsubmodule bump PR exists.\\n\\n---\\n\\nSecurity reporting → [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).\\n\",\"encoding\":\"utf-8\",\"sha\":\"7a48efb162ba25ac0558bfca52063874eed6b9d3\",\"display_url\":\"https://github.com/prime-radiant-inc/superpowers-evals/blob/main/README.md\",\"display_title\":\"README.md\"}"

    "text": "{\"content\":\"# Security Policy\\n\\nquorum is a security-sensitive eval runner. It can launch real agent CLIs in\\npermissive modes, collect transcripts, read local session logs, and send run\\nartifacts to an LLM verifier.\\n\\n## Reporting a Vulnerability\\n\\nDo not file public issues or pull requests containing exploitable details,\\ntokens, session logs with secrets, or private filesystem paths.\\n\\nReport security issues privately through GitHub private vulnerability reporting\\nwhen available. If that is not available, contact a Prime Radiant maintainer\\nprivately and share only the minimum detail needed to reproduce the issue.\\n\\n## Sensitive Data\\n\\nNever commit:\\n\\n- `.env` files or API keys\\n- `results/` output\\n- raw `session.log`, `trajectory.json`, `filesystem.json`, `verdict.json`, or\\n  `meta.json` from real runs\\n- local agent config directories such as `.claude/`, `.codex/`, or `.gemini/`\\n\\nIf a real run prints or captures a secret, rotate the secret before sharing any\\nartifact derived from that run.\\n\\n## CI Boundary\\n\\nPublic CI must run only static/unit checks. Live `quorum run ...` evals require\\ncredentials and permissive agent CLIs, so they belong in trusted local or\\nmaintainer-controlled environments only.\\n\\n## Kimi Live Evals\\n\\nKimi live evals launch `kimi --yolo` inside a Quorum-prepared workdir. Quorum\\nuses a fresh `KIMI_CODE_HOME`, installs only the local Superpowers plugin via\\n`plugins/installed.json`, and supplies model auth through a temporary runtime\\nenv file that is deleted by the launcher and runner cleanup paths.\\n\\nTreat Kimi `results/` artifacts as sensitive. Raw `wire.jsonl` logs may contain\\nmodel outputs, tool arguments, and provider env until Kimi tool-subprocess env\\nscrubbing is verified. Do not run live Kimi evals in public CI or against\\nuntrusted PR scenarios.\\n\",\"encoding\":\"utf-8\",\"sha\":\"8747e49c2a661e46b8ada67f677bca8b5c4d51a3\",\"display_url\":\"https://github.com/prime-radiant-inc/superpowers-evals/blob/main/SECURITY.md\",\"display_title\":\"SECURITY.md\"}"
