    "text": "```\n$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)/../.keel/runtime/relay/<task-id>/\n├── session.json                    # 참여 역할, 현재 게이트, 시작 시각\n├── 001-pm-plan.md                  # 아티팩트 (추가전용, 절대 수정 안 함)\n├── 001-pm-plan.md.sha256           # 무결성\n├── 001-pm-plan.md.ready            # ← 이게 있어야 \"게시됨\". 유일한 발행 마커\n├── 002-implementer-report.md(+…)\n├── 003-security-review.md(+…)\n└── .heartbeat/<role>-<session>     # 생존 신호\n```\n\n**게시 절차** (중간에 죽어도 안전): 임시파일 작성 → fsync → rename → sha256 기록 → `.ready` 생성\n**소비 절차**: `.ready`가 있는 것만 읽음 → sha256 검증 → 처리\n\n**아티팩트 종류**: `plan` · `review` · `fix` · `question` · `decision` · `note`\n\n**승인 게이트는 fail-closed**: 최종 승인은 `kind: decision` + 작성자가 리드가 아닌 것 + 검토 대상 커밋 해시가 일치할 때만 성립합니다. 이게 없으면 에이전트가 자기 작업을 자기가 승인해버립니다.\n\n**역할 스왑 규칙**: 한 게이트에서 2라운드 연속 blocker가 나오면 역할을 교대하거나 사람에게 에스컬레이션합니다.\n\n### 11-3. Orca와의 접합\n\nOrca가 제공하는 것을 최대한 쓰되, **의존하지 않게** 만듭니다.\n\n| 필요 | Orca가 주면 | 없으면 (폴백) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 역할별 격리 실행 | 워크트리별 세션 | `git worktree add` 직접 |\n| 세션 간 알림 | 인박스 메시지(`@claude`/`@codex`) | `.ready` 파일 폴링 (5초) |\n| 태스크 의존성 | Runs→Tasks DAG | `session.json`의 게이트 순서 |\n| 리뷰·머지 | 리뷰 큐 | `keel ship` (테스트 → PR) |\n\n`.agents/tools/relay.sh` 안에서 `orca` 명령 존재 여부를 감지해 분기합니다. Orca를 빼도, 다른 실행기로 바꿔도 하네스는 그대로 돕니다. **프로젝트마다 붙여 쓰려면 이 독립성이 필수입니다.**\n\n> Orca 판별이 필요합니다: `onorca.dev`(Stably ADE)와 `orca-cli/orca`(Go, 커밋 5개 스켈레톤)는 **이름만 같은 다른 제품**입니다. 후자라면 위 표의 \"Orca가 주면\" 열이 전부 폴백으로 내려갑니다.\n\n---\n\n## 12. 검증 — `keel verify`가 실제로 검사하는 것\n\n```\nkeel verify [--scope docs|code|all]\n\n문서 계층\n  D1  프론트매터 스키마 (필수 필드 · enum 값 유효성)\n  D2  lifecycle 전이 규칙 (draft에서 verified로 점프 불가 등)\n  D3  ID 중복·누락, 파일명 ↔ id 일치\n  D4  sources/binds/supersedes 링크 무결성 (죽은 참조 0)\n  D5  고아 문서 (INDEX.md에 없거나, 어떤 문서도 참조하지 않는 것)\n  D6  폴더 규약 위반 (타입에 맞지 않는 경로)\n\n드리프트\n  R1  binds.code 경로가 변경된 커밋 존재 && 문서 updated가 그 이전  → sync=drifted\n  R2  authority=binding && sync=drifted 인 문서 존재                → 게이트 실패\n  R3  ARCHITECTURE의 AD-n 규칙과 충돌하는 구현 패턴 (rules/ glob 기반)\n\n스펙 ↔ 코드\n  S1  각 AC에 대응하는 테스트가 존재하는가 (AC id를 테스트명/주석에 요구)\n  S2  커밋 트레일러 Spec: 가 붙지 않은 기능 커밋\n  S3  SPEC에 없는 신규 공개 API/라우트\n\n하네스\n  H1  .claude/·.codex/ 투영이 .agents/와 동기 상태인가 (심링크 끊김 검사)\n  H2  Codex 프로젝트 신뢰 여부\n  H3  훅 등록 상태, 매처 어휘 정합성\n\n출력: 통과 / 실패 / **미검증**  ← 확인 못 한 항목은 통과로 처리하지 않는다\n```\n\nCI와 로컬 프리커밋 양쪽에서 돕니다. 다만 **차단은 D1~D4·R2만**, 나머지는 경고로 시작하세요. 처음부터 전부 차단하면 하네스를 꺼버리게 됩니다.\n\n---\n\n## 13. 자기학습 — 두 층위로 분리\n\n두 가지를 섞으면 안 됩니다. **프로젝트가 배우는 것**과 **하네스가 배우는 것**은 저장 위치도 수명도 다릅니다.\n\n### 13-1. 층위 A — 프로젝트 학습 (`docs/90-learning/`)\n\n**캡처 트리거** (자동):\n\n- `keel verify` 게이트 실패\n- 같은 오류로 수정 3회 실패\n- 롤백 발생\n- 리뷰에서 2라운드 연속 blocker\n- 배포/운영 장애\n\n**기록 포맷** (`TROUBLESHOOTING.md` 추가전용 항목):\n\n```markdown\n### 2026-08-04 · 세션 배포 후 로그인 무한 리다이렉트\n- 증상(원문 보존): <에러 원문>\n- 마지막 정상 상태: <커밋 해시 / 시각>\n- 분류: 계정 / 권한 / 설정 / 코드 / 데이터 / 외부서비스   ← 하나만\n- 가설과 근거: 1) … 2) …\n- 실행한 진단(가장 작은 것 하나): …\n- 변경한 원인 후보(하나만): …\n- 결과: 해결 / 실패(되돌림)\n- 재발 방지: <규칙·테스트·문서 중 무엇을 바꿨나>\n```\n\n운영 규칙에 쓰신 \"오류 원문 보존 → 마지막 정상 상태 → 분류 → 우선순위 → 가장 작은 진단 하나 → 원인 후보 하나만 변경 → 재검증\"을 그대로 포맷으로 굳혔습니다. **같은 해결책을 반복하지 않기 위한 장치**입니다.\n\n**환류**: `LESSON-nnnn`이 `lifecycle: open`으로 쌓이면, 다음 사이클 시작 시 `retrospector`가 관련 lesson을 읽어 계획에 반영하고 `applied`로 바꿉니다.\n\n### 13-2. 층위 B — 하네스 학습 (`.agents/`)\n\nSuperpowers의 \"스킬 작성 = TDD\"를 그대로 씁니다.\n\n```\n실패 관찰\n   ↓  실패한 상황과 에이전트의 합리화 문장을 그대로 기록\nRED   : 스킬 없이 그 시나리오를 재현 → 실패하는 것을 확인\nGREEN : 관찰된 그 합리화만 반박하는 최소 문구를 스킬에 추가\n검증  : 같은 시나리오 재실행 → 규칙을 지키는지 확인\nREFACTOR: 새로운 회피 논리가 나오면 반박 추가\n```\n\n**핵심 기법 — 메타 질문**: 에이전트가 스킬을 읽고도 어겼을 때 직접 물어봅니다.\n\n> \"스킬을 읽고도 C를 골랐습니다. 이 스킬이 어떻게 쓰여 있었다면 A만이 정답이라는 게 명확했을까요?\"\n\n답이 세 부류로 갈립니다: ① 명확했는데 무시했다 → 상위 원칙 문구 강화 ② \"X라고 썼어야 한다\" → 그 문장 그대로 추가 ③ \"그 섹션을 못 봤다\" → 구조 재배치.\n\n**`.agents/CHANGELOG.md`** 에 모든 스킬 변경을 \"관찰된 실패 → 근본 원인 → 수정\" 형식으로 기록합니다. 기능 변경 목록이 아니라 **사후 분석 로그**로 씁니다.\n\n**보호 규칙**: 반박표·Red Flags 같이 정교하게 튜닝된 내용은 **근거(재현 시나리오) 없이 수정 금지**. 안 그러면 학습한 것이 정리 작업 중에 소실됩니다.\n\n---\n\n## 14. AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md 최신화 · 자동 폴더링 · 신규 자산 생성\n\n### 14-1. AGENTS.md는 짧아야 한다\n\n**100줄 이내**를 상한으로 둡니다. 길어지면 아무도(에이전트 포함) 안 읽습니다. 내용은 **규칙 요약 + 어디를 읽어야 하는지 포인터**만.\n\n```markdown\n# <프로젝트명>\n\n<!-- keel:auto:begin — 이 블록은 keel sync가 재생성합니다. 직접 수정 금지 -->\n## 스택\nNext.js 15 · TypeScript · Postgres(Supabase) · Vercel\n\n## 자주 쓰는 명령\n개발 `pnpm dev` · 테스트 `pnpm test` · 린트 `pnpm lint` · 검증 `keel verify`\n\n## 지금 살아 있는 SSOT 문서\n- 아키텍처 spine: docs/30-architecture/ARCHITECTURE.md (active, matches-code, 2026-08-01)\n- 진행 중 스펙: SPEC-0011 소셜 로그인 (in-progress)\n- 최근 결정: ADR-0003 세션 저장 방식 (accepted, 2026-07-28)\n<!-- keel:auto:end -->\n\n## 규칙\n1. 테스트 없이 프로덕션 코드 없음\n2. 근본 원인 없이 수정 없음\n3. 증거 없이 완료 선언 없음 — 확인 못 한 건 '미검증'\n4. 문서는 `keel doc new`로만 생성\n5. SPEC에 없는 걸 만들지 않는다. 필요하면 SPEC을 먼저 고친다\n\n## 더 읽을 곳\n전체 문서 색인 `docs/INDEX.md` · 역할 정의 `.agents/roles/` · 워크플로우 `.agents/workflows/`\n```\n\n**갱신 방식**: 마커 블록 안쪽만 `keel sync`가 재생성. 바깥은 사람이 씁니다. 이 분리가 없으면 자동 갱신이 사람이 쓴 내용을 지웁니다.\n\n**갱신 시점**: ① PostToolUse 훅이 `docs/**` 또는 `package.json` 변경 감지 ② 세션 종료 시 ③ CI에서 `keel sync --check`로 드리프트 검출.\n\n`CLAUDE.md`는 `AGENTS.md`의 심볼릭 링크이므로 자동으로 같이 갱신됩니다.\n\n### 14-2. 자동 폴더링\n\n문서는 **반드시** `keel doc new <type> [--feature <f>]`로 만듭니다. 이 명령이 하는 일:\n\n1. 타입 → 경로 결정 (`.agents/policies/doc-routing.yaml`)\n2. ID 채번 (타입별 연번, 충돌 없음)\n3. 템플릿 + 프론트매터 주입 (created/updated/lifecycle 초기값)\n4. `docs/INDEX.md` 갱신\n5. 상위 문서의 `binds`에 역참조 추가\n\n**강제**: PostToolUse 훅이 `docs/` 밑에 규약 밖 파일이 생기면 경고하고 올바른 경로를 제안합니다. 차단이 아니라 제안으로 시작하세요 — 예외 상황에서 막히면 사람이 하네스를 끕니다.\n\n### 14-3. 프로젝트 도중 신규 스킬·역할 생성\n\n`.agents/workflows/forge/` — 하네스 규칙에 맞는 새 자산을 만드는 워크플로우.\n\n```\n1. 정말 새 스킬이 필요한가?\n   · 기존 스킬 수정으로 되는가? → 수정 (스킬 증식은 발견율을 떨어뜨림)\n   · 1회성인가? → 그냥 그때 지시로 처리\n2. 유형 판정: skill(지식·절차) / role(페르소나·책임) / workflow(단계형) / rule(glob 트리거)\n3. 규약 준수 생성\n   · description은 \"언제 쓰는지\"만. \"무엇을 하는지\" 요약 금지\n   · 본문에 구체 도구명 금지 (동작 언어만)\n   · 병렬은 \"선택적 가속\" 마커로만\n4. RED 검증: 이 스킬 없이 실패하는 시나리오를 먼저 확인\n5. GREEN 검증: 스킬 적용 후 통과 확인\n6. keel sync → 양쪽 런타임에 투영\n7. .agents/CHANGELOG.md에 \"관찰된 실패 → 수정\" 기록\n```\n\n4·5번이 없으면 아무도 안 읽는 스킬이 쌓입니다. **RED 없이 만든 스킬은 커밋하지 않습니다.**\n\n### 14-4. MCP 사전 준비\n\n`.agents/keel.yaml`에 역할별 권장 MCP를 선언하고 `.mcp.json`을 생성합니다.\n\n```yaml\nmcp:\n  shared: [filesystem, git]\n  by_role:\n    architect:         [context7]           # 라이브러리 최신 문서\n    implementer:       [context7]\n    ux-architect:      [figma]\n    creative-director: [figma]\n    reviewer-security: []                   # 외부 접근 최소화\n    devops:            []                   # 인프라 MCP는 명시 승인 후에만\n```\n\n**원칙**: 검토자와 운영 역할에는 도구를 최소로 줍니다. 권한이 넓을수록 사고 범위가 넓어집니다. 인프라·과금·배포에 닿는 MCP는 운영 규칙 7번(위험한 변경)에 따라 **명시적 승인 없이 붙이지 않습니다.**\n\n---\n\n## 15. 도입 로드맵 — 작은 단위로, 각 단계마다 검증\n\n한 번에 다 만들면 실패 원인을 못 찾습니다. 각 단계는 **그 단계만으로도 쓸모가 있어야** 하고, 명확한 검증 기준을 가집니다.\n\n| 단계 | 만드는 것 | 검증 기준 (관찰 가능한 것) | 대략 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **P0** 문서 표준 | `docs/` 폴더 구조 · 프론트매터 스키마 · 상태 모델 · `doc-routing.yaml` · 템플릿 8종 | 기존 프로젝트 문서 3개를 새 포맷으로 옮겼을 때 필드가 전부 채워진다 | 0.5일 |\n| **P1** SSOT 골격 | `.agents/` 트리 · 심링크 투영 · `AGENTS.md`↔`CLAUDE.md` · `keel sync`/`doctor` | `claude`와 `codex`를 각각 띄웠을 때 **같은 스킬 목록**이 보인다. Codex trust 통과 확인 | 0.5일 |\n| **P2** 부트스트랩 + 3 Iron Law | `bootstrap/SKILL.md` · SessionStart 훅(+AGENTS.md 폴백) | 양쪽 CLI 새 세션에서 \"지금 적용 중인 규칙\"을 물었을 때 3개를 정확히 답한다 | 0.5일 |\n| **P3** 기획 트랙 | intake 라우터 · PRD 템플릿(3프로파일) · ADR 템플릿 · 백로그 | 요구 5건(난이도 다양)을 넣어 라우터가 예상 트랙을 고르고 근거를 제시한다 | 1~2일 |\n| **P4** 문서 검증 | `keel verify` D1~D6 · `docs/INDEX.md` 자동 생성 | 일부러 깨진 문서(죽은 링크·잘못된 전이·고아)를 심었을 때 전부 잡는다 | 1일 |\n| **P5** 개발 트랙 | 역할 12개 · plan 템플릿 · 리뷰/조정 게이트 · 진행 원장 | 작은 기능 1건을 SPEC→PLAN→구현→검토→검증까지 끝까지 통과시킨다 | 2일 |\n| **P6** 릴레이 | `.keel/runtime/relay/` 프로토콜 · Orca 감지 분기 | Claude 세션이 쓴 계획을 Codex 세션이 읽고 리뷰를 게시, 다시 Claude가 소비한다 | 1~2일 |\n| **P7** 드리프트 감지 | `binds.code` 기반 R1~R3 | 코드만 고치고 문서를 안 고쳤을 때 `sync: drifted`로 자동 강등된다 | 1일 |\n| **P8** 디자인 트랙 | 역할 3개 · 안티패턴 정책 · 체크리스트 게이트 | 랜딩 1개를 만들어 체크리스트 전 항목 통과/예외사유 기록이 남는다 | 1일 |\n| **P9** CIS 이식 | 워크플로우 5개 + methods CSV | T3 요구 1건을 CIS→브리프→PRD-Full까지 통과 | 1~2일 |\n| **P10** 학습 루프 | 캡처 트리거 · LESSON 환류 · `forge` 워크플로우 | 실패 1건을 일부러 만들어 LESSON이 자동 생성되고 다음 계획에 반영된다 | 1일 |\n\n**P0~P2까지만 해도 즉시 쓸모가 있습니다.** 거기서 멈춰도 \"두 CLI가 같은 규칙을 본다\"는 목표는 달성됩니다. P3 이후는 필요할 때 붙이세요.\n\n---\n\n## 16. 위험과 주의\n\n| 위험 | 영향 | 대응 |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Codex 프로젝트 미신뢰** | `.codex/` 설정·훅이 조용히 무시됨. 원인 파악이 어려움 | `keel doctor`의 첫 항목으로 검사. 실패 시 명확한 안내 문구 |\n| **훅 매처 어휘 차이** | Claude 매처를 복붙하면 Codex에서 무동작 | 생성기가 런타임별 매처를 따로 씀. 같은 스크립트를 호출 |\n| **Codex 서브에이전트 부재** | 병렬 검토 워크플로우가 한쪽에서만 동작 | 5장 규약(선택적 가속). 병렬을 필수 경로에 넣지 않음 |\n| **심링크가 깨짐** (git이 심링크를 파일로 체크아웃하는 설정 등) | 스킬이 조용히 사라짐 | `keel doctor` H1에서 검사. `.claude/`·`.codex/`는 gitignore하고 `keel sync`로 재생성 |\n| **워크트리 간 상태 분리** | 릴레이 메시지를 서로 못 봄 | `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` 기준 절대경로 (2장) |\n| **BMAD 상표** | 브랜드명 사용 시 라이선스 위반 | `bmad-` 접두어·브랜드명 미사용. 코드/구조만 MIT로 차용, 출처 표기 |\n| **스킬 증식** | 20개를 넘으면 발견율이 급락 | `forge` 워크플로우 1단계에서 \"기존 수정으로 되는가\"를 먼저 물음. 상한 25개 |\n| **검증 과잉 차단** | 하네스를 꺼버리게 됨 | 차단은 D1~D4·R2만. 나머지는 경고로 시작 |\n| **문서 상태 수기 관리 붕괴** | SSOT 판정 불가 | `sync`는 사람이 아니라 `keel verify`만 씀. 수기 변경 금지 |\n| **비용 폭증** | opus를 모든 역할에 상속 | `model_must_be_explicit: true`. 기계적 작업은 하위 모델 |\n\n**명시 승인 없이 진행하지 않을 것** (운영 규칙 7번 그대로): 결제·유료 플랜, 약관 동의, 저장소/DB 삭제, 네임서버 변경, 저장소 공개 전환, 소유권 이전, 광범위 권한 부여, 운영 데이터 초기화, 시크릿 교체, force push·히스토리 재작성. 하네스에서는 이 목록을 `.agents/policies/require-approval.md`로 두고 부트스트랩에서 참조합니다.\n\n"

Resolve `<skill-dir>` to this skill's actual directory; in copied local eval workdirs this is usually `.codex/skills/openai-docs`:\n\n```bash\nnode <skill-dir>/scripts/fetch-codex-manual.mjs\n```\n\nIf you need to override the cache dir, pass `--cache-dir <cache-dir>`. On Windows, the helper checks `%TEMP%` and `%TMP%` automatically; in PowerShell, `$env:TEMP\\\\openai-docs-cache` is a typical explicit override.\n\nTreat helper availability as established by explicit read-only/no-shell policy or an actual command result. A guessed sandbox or guessed helper failure is not enough to switch to Docs MCP or web lookup; after an actual helper command failure, continue to the narrowest official next source below.\n\nThe helper verifies freshness, writes `codex-manual.md`, and emits `codex-manual.outline.md`. The outline maps source pages and headings to line ranges; use it to choose the relevant manual section, then read or search targeted manual sections for Codex product facts. Use the skill directory to locate and run the helper; after the helper succeeds, use the returned manual and outline paths as the search scope for Codex product facts and term coverage checks.\n\nReuse the same-thread manual and outline paths for follow-up Codex questions. Refresh first when the manual was fetched more than about a day ago, the path is unusable, the path came from another thread or uncertain provenance, or likely-current information is missing and staleness is plausible.\n\nFor questions about whether the manual is current enough to rely on now, run the helper when temp caching is allowed and base the answer on its returned status, manual path, and outline path.\n\nIf the manual resolves a Codex claim, answer from it and stop expanding sources for that claim; continue the user's broader task if the docs lookup was only one dependency. Manual source pages and known anchors are enough citation support for manual-covered material.\n\nIf the helper is skipped because the session is read-only, has no shell execution, or has no allowed temp cache, the next source is Docs MCP: call `mcp__openaiDeveloperDocs__search_openai_docs`, then `mcp__openaiDeveloperDocs__fetch_openai_doc` for a relevant hit before any web fallback.\n\nIf a user names a Codex term or mode that a fresh manual does not use, search the manual for obvious adjacent concepts, then answer that the exact term is not documented and use the closest documented terminology. If the prompt asks how that term maps to Codex behavior, resolve the mapping from adjacent manual sections. If the exact term remains material or likely current after that manual pass, use one narrow Docs MCP search/fetch before bounded uncertainty; otherwise, the source lookup for that terminology or mapping claim is complete.\n\nUse the narrowest official next source only when the manual is unavailable, the helper fails, temp caching is not allowed, another material claim is missing or likely stale, or the user explicitly needs a page-specific citation. Prefer one specific Docs MCP search and, if it returns a clearly relevant page, one fetch; for unresolved Codex capability names, acronyms, scheduling terms, or exact error text, this Docs MCP step is the next source before web search. After the manual plus any permitted Docs MCP gap-fill, resolve remaining gaps as bounded uncertainty. Use official-domain web fallback only after that Docs MCP path is unavailable or unhelpful. If the claim is still not established, stop with bounded uncertainty. If official docs/manual conflict with a callable capability already surfaced in the current session, state the conflict and prefer verified current-session behavior for that environment.\n\nFor undocumented or private-looking model slugs, product mode labels, entitlement labels, account access paths, or rollout names, answer from current public docs and bounded uncertainty. Those labels are not a reason to leave the public source route.\n\nFor support-style diagnostics, prefer a layer-by-layer answer from the manual over provider-specific web lookups: installed/enabled plugin, bundled app or connector authorization, MCP setup, workspace/admin policy, restart or new-thread expectations, then support or feedback if still unresolved.\n\nIf the source route still does not establish a claim, return bounded uncertainty or route to support, an admin, or product feedback instead of widening the investigation.\n\nFor unresolved product terminology, answer from the manual plus the allowed official next source. If those sources do not establish the term, answer with bounded uncertainty from those sources.\n\n### Surface Map\n\nWhen Codex nouns or durable-instruction surfaces overlap, recommend the smallest surface that matches the scope:\n\n- Prompt or thread context -> one-off task constraints.\n- `AGENTS.md` -> durable repo conventions, commands, verification steps, and review expectations; closer nested files apply under their subtree.\n- Project `.codex/config.toml` -> trusted-repo Codex settings such as sandbox, MCP, hooks, model, or reasoning defaults.\n- Global config or global guidance -> personal defaults across repos.\n- Skill -> reusable task workflow with references or scripts.\n- Plugin -> installable bundle with skills plus commands, tools, MCP config, hooks, assets, apps, or marketplace metadata.\n- MCP server or app connector -> live external data/actions or authorized private app/workspace data. Use connectors for private Google Docs, Calendar, Slack, GitHub, Notion, and similar data instead of web search or model memory.\n- Automation -> scheduled checks, reminders, monitors, or follow-up work; use a thread heartbeat when continuity in an existing thread matters.\n- Hook -> lifecycle enforcement around tool calls, commands, or file edits.\n\nSplit mixed-scope requests instead of forcing one answer. Example: \"always do X, but only for this PR\" defaults to prompt/thread context for the current run; use `AGENTS.md` or project config only if it should persist, hooks only for mechanical enforcement, and automations only for scheduled or follow-up work.\n\nUse this quick product map when needed: CLI is terminal-first local repo work; IDE extension is editor-attached coding; Codex app is desktop planning, review, and interactive work; cloud/web is hosted parallel/offloaded work; Browser Use/in-app browser is Codex-controlled web testing; Chrome extension uses the user's Chrome profile; Computer Use controls desktop apps and OS UI. Keep `config.toml` defaults, `requirements.toml` constraints, and managed/admin policy separate.\n\n### Boundaries And Output\n\n- API key auth does not imply ChatGPT, cloud task, or connector access. For plugin/app/auth failures, check bundle availability, plugin installed/enabled state, connector/app authorization, MCP setup, restart/refresh expectations, workspace policy, and per-surface availability before answering.\n- Sandbox or network denials need scoped escalation with a clear justification. Destructive commands, writes outside the workspace, or broad access changes require explicit approval.\n- Memory can provide user preference or context, but explicit prompt instructions win and memory is not a source for current external facts.\n- For affirmative surface-selection answers, use this shape: recommendation, why, what to avoid, and the manual/source evidence used.\n- When page-specific Codex citations are actually needed, these anchors often fit: `concepts/customization#agents-guidance` for `AGENTS.md`, `concepts/customization#skills` for skills, `plugins/build#plugin-structure` for plugins, `concepts/customization#mcp` for MCP, `config-advanced#hooks` for hooks, `app/automations#thread-automations` for thread automations, and `config-reference#configtoml` for config.\n\n## If MCP server is missing\n\nIf MCP tools fail or no OpenAI docs resources are available:\n\n1. Run the install command yourself: `codex mcp add openaiDeveloperDocs --url https://developers.openai.com/mcp`\n2. If it fails due to permissions/sandboxing, immediately retry the same command with escalated permissions and include a 1-sentence justification for approval.\n3. Ask the user to run the install command only if the escalated attempt fails.\n4. Ask the user to restart Codex.\n5. Re-run the doc search/fetch after restart.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Clarify whether the request is general docs lookup, model selection, a model-string upgrade, prompt-upgrade guidance, or broader API/provider migration.\n2. For Codex self-knowledge requests, follow the Codex self-knowledge source procedure above.\n3. For model-selection or upgrade requests, prefer current remote docs over bundled references when the user asks for latest/current/default guidance.\n   - For docs-only model-selection questions, fetch `https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/latest-model.md`, find the latest model ID and explicit migration or prompt-guidance links, and prefer explicit links over derived URLs.\n   - For explicit named-model requests, preserve the requested model target. Mention newer remote guidance only as optional.\n   - For latest/current/default prompting guidance or dynamic upgrades, run the resolver with the platform-specific command above as the first docs operation before any direct latest-model.md fetch or API-key credential gate, then fetch both returned guide URLs directly when possible.\n   - Fetch returned guide URLs exactly; do not derive or substitute model-specific URLs.\n   - If a direct guide fetch fails or returns only a title, use the developer-docs MCP tools or official OpenAI-domain search to find the same guide content.\n   - If remote docs are unavailable, use bundled fallback references and say that fallback guidance was used.\n4. For model upgrades, keep changes behavior-preserving and scoped: update active OpenAI API model defaults, directly related prompts, and the registries, routing, pricing, capability, or picker surfaces that the user actually placed in scope.\n5. Leave historical docs, examples, eval baselines, fixtures, provider comparisons, intentionally pinned fallbacks, and ambiguous older model usage unchanged unless the user explicitly asks to upgrade them. Do not collapse a multi-model router or picker into one flagship model; preserve the existing cost, latency, and quality roles.\n6. Keep SDK, tooling, IDE, plugin, shell, auth, and provider-environment migrations out of a model-and-prompt upgrade unless the user explicitly asks for them.\n7. If a safe upgrade needs API-surface changes, schema rewiring, tool-handler changes, or broader implementation work, classify it explicitly. Make those changes when implementation is within the user's requested scope; otherwise report the exact blocker or confirmation needed instead of silently changing behavior.\n8. For general docs lookup, start with a compact, title-like search query of 2-6 essential terms. Do not turn the full user question into a keyword list. Fetch the best page and exact section needed, and answer with concise citations.\n\n## Reference map\n\nRead only what you need:\n\n- `https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/latest-model.md` -> current model-selection and \"best/latest/current model\" questions.\n- `scripts/fetch-codex-manual.mjs` -> current Codex manual fetch, verification, local temp cache, and outline generation.\n- `https://developers.openai.com/codex/codex-manual.md` -> current Codex self-knowledge synthesis, including setup, customization, skills, plugins, MCP, hooks, `AGENTS.md`, automations, and surface behavior; normally access it through the helper path and targeted file reads when temp caching is available.\n- `references/latest-model.md` -> bundled fallback for model-selection and \"best/latest/current model\" questions.\n- `references/upgrade-guide.md` -> bundled routing fallback for model upgrade and upgrade-planning requests.\n- `references/upgrading-to-gpt-5p6-sol.md` -> GPT-5.6 Sol/family migration judgment, compatibility gates, optional feature boundaries, and validation.\n- `references/prompting-guide.md` -> bundled GPT-5.6 prompting fallback plus the live Prompting Best Practices extraction contract.\n\n## Quality rules\n\n- Treat OpenAI docs as the source of truth; avoid speculation.\n- For Codex self-knowledge, follow the source route above instead of relying on remembered behavior.\n- Keep migration changes narrow and behavior-preserving.\n- Prefer prompt-only upgrades when possible.\n- Avoid inventing pricing, availability, parameters, API changes, or breaking changes.\n- Keep quotes short and within policy limits; prefer paraphrase with citations.\n- If multiple pages differ, call out the difference and cite both.\n- If official docs and verified callable current-session behavior disagree, state the conflict before making broad claims or edits.\n- If docs do not cover the user’s need, say so and offer next steps.\n\n## Tooling notes\n\n- Use MCP doc tools before web search for OpenAI-related markdown docs. The Codex manual flow is the exception: follow the Codex self-knowledge source procedure for broad Codex synthesis.\n- If the MCP server is installed but returns no meaningful results, then use web search as a fallback.\n- When falling back to web search, restrict to official OpenAI domains (developers.openai.com, platform.openai.com) and cite sources.\n"
  }

If you flood the main chat (where you're defining requirements, constraints, and decisions) with noisy intermediate output such as exploration notes, test logs, stack traces, and command output, the session can become less reliable over time.\n\nThis is often described as:\n\n- **Context pollution**: useful information gets buried under noisy intermediate output.\n- **Context rot**: performance degrades as the chat fills up with less relevant details.\n\nFor background, see the Chroma writeup on [context rot](https://research.trychroma.com/context-rot).\n\nSubagent workflows help by moving noisy work off the main thread:\n\n- Keep the **main agent** focused on requirements, decisions, and final outputs.\n- Run specialized **subagents** in parallel for exploration, tests, or log analysis.\n- Return **summaries** from subagents instead of raw intermediate output.\n\nThey can also save time when the work can run independently in parallel, and\nthey make larger-shaped tasks more tractable by breaking them into bounded\npieces. Supported clients let you open these threads to inspect progress or results.\n\n#### Triggering subagent workflows\n\nAt most intelligence levels, ask for subagents or parallel agent work\ndirectly. The approval overlay\nshows the source thread label, and you can press `o` to open that thread before\nyou approve, reject, or answer the request.\n\nIn non-interactive flows, or whenever a run can't surface a fresh approval, an\naction that needs new approval fails and Codex surfaces the error back to the\nparent workflow.\n\nCodex also reapplies the parent turn's live runtime overrides when it spawns a\nchild. Choose\nthe permission mode for the parent turn before you ask Codex to delegate work.\n\nYou can also override the sandbox configuration for individual [custom agents](#custom-agents), such as explicitly marking one to work in read-only mode.\n\n#### Custom agents\n\nCodex ships with built-in agents:\n\n- `default`: general-purpose fallback agent.\n- `worker`: execution-focused agent for implementation and fixes.\n- `explorer`: read-heavy codebase exploration agent.\n\nTo define your own custom agents, add standalone TOML files under\n`~/.codex/agents/` for personal agents or `.codex/agents/` for project-scoped\nagents.\n\nEach file defines one custom agent. Codex loads these files as configuration\nlayers for spawned sessions, so custom agents can override the same settings as\na normal Codex session config. That can feel heavier than a dedicated agent\nmanifest, and the format may evolve as authoring and sharing mature.\n\nEvery standalone custom agent file must define:\n\n- `name`\n- `description`\n- `developer_instructions`\n\nIf a custom agent file sets `model` or `model_reasoning_effort`, the value in\nthe file takes precedence. Otherwise, Codex resolves each setting independently:\nan explicit spawn value, then the corresponding `[agents]` default, then the\nparent's value. If a spawn selects a different model and neither an explicit nor\nconfigured effort is present, Codex uses that model's default effort. Other\nsession settings, such as `sandbox_mode`, `mcp_servers`, and `skills.config`,\ninherit from the parent when the custom agent file omits them.\n\n#### Global settings\n\nGlobal subagent settings still live under `[agents]` in your [configuration](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-basic#configuration-precedence).\n\n| Field                                       | Type    | Required | Purpose                                                             |\n| ------------------------------------------- | ------- | :------: | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `agents.enabled`                            | boolean |    No    | Enable or disable multi-agent tools.                                |\n| `agents.max_concurrent_threads_per_session` | number  |    No    | Cap concurrently open spawned-agent threads, excluding the primary. |\n| `agents.default_subagent_model`             | string  |    No    | Set the default model for spawned agents.                           |\n| `agents.default_subagent_reasoning_effort`  | string  |    No    | Set the default reasoning effort for spawned agents.                |\n| `agents.interrupt_message`                  | boolean |    No    | Record a model-visible message when an agent turn is interrupted.   |\n\n**Notes:**\n\n- `agents.enabled` defaults to `true`. Set it to `false` to disable multi-agent tools.\n- When you leave `agents.max_concurrent_threads_per_session` unset, Codex chooses the default. Existing configurations can keep using `agents.max_threads` as a legacy alias.\n- Explicit spawn values override `agents.default_subagent_model` and `agents.default_subagent_reasoning_effort`.\n- `agents.interrupt_message` defaults to `true`. Set it to `false` to omit the model-visible interruption message from the agent's context.\n- If a custom agent name matches a built-in agent such as `explorer`, your custom agent takes precedence.\n\n#### Custom agent file schema\n\n| Field                    | Type   | Required | Purpose                                                         |\n| ------------------------ | ------ | :------: | --------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `name`                   | string |   Yes    | Agent name Codex uses when spawning or referring to this agent. |\n| `description`            | string |   Yes    | Human-facing guidance for when Codex should use this agent.     |\n| `developer_instructions` | string |   Yes    | Core instructions that define the agent's behavior.             |\n\nYou can also include other supported `config.toml` keys in a custom agent file, such as `model`, `model_reasoning_effort`, `sandbox_mode`, `mcp_servers`, and `skills.config`.\n\n\n### Configuration Reference\n\nSource: [Configuration Reference](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-reference.md)\n\nUse this page as a searchable reference for Codex configuration files. For conceptual guidance and examples, start with [Config basics](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-basic) and [Advanced Config](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-advanced).\n\n#### `config.toml`\n\nUser-level configuration lives in `~/.codex/config.toml`. You can also add project-scoped overrides in `.codex/config.toml` files. Codex loads project-scoped config files only when you trust the project.\n\nProject-scoped config can't override machine-local provider, auth,\nhost-owned app request metadata, notification, configuration profile selection,\nor telemetry routing keys. Codex ignores `openai_base_url`,\n`chatgpt_base_url`, `apps_mcp_product_sku`, `model_provider`,\n`model_providers`, `notify`, `profile`, `profiles`,\n`experimental_realtime_ws_base_url`, and `otel` when they appear in a\nproject-local `.codex/config.toml`; put provider, notification, and telemetry\nkeys in user-level config instead. Config [profile files](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-advanced#profiles) live next to\n`config.toml` as `$CODEX_HOME/profile-name.config.toml`; select one with\n`--profile profile-name`.\n\nFor sandbox and approval keys (`approval_policy`, `sandbox_mode`, and `sandbox_workspace_write.*`), pair this reference with [Sandbox and approvals](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security#sandbox-and-approvals), [Protected paths in writable roots](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security#protected-paths-in-writable-roots), and [Network access](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security#network-access). For beta permission profiles, see [Permissions](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/permissions).\n\n| Key                                                           | Type / Values                                                                                                                                                              | Default | Details                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |\n| ------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `agents`                                                      | `table`                                                                                                                                                                    |         | Multi-agent settings and custom role declarations. Scalar setting names are reserved and can't be used as custom role names.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |\n| `agents..config_file`                                         | `string (path)`                                                                                                                                                            |         | Path to a TOML config layer for that role; relative paths resolve from the config file that declares the role.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |\n| `agents..description`                                         | `string`                                                                                                                                                                   |         | Role guidance shown to Codex when choosing and spawning that agent type.                                                                                                                                                                                                        …17291 tokens truncated…se file-based credential storage) or your OS keychain/keyring\n- `history.jsonl` (if history persistence is enabled)\n- Other per-user state such as logs and caches\n\nFor authentication details (including credential storage modes), see [Authentication](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/auth). For the full list of configuration keys, see [Configuration Reference](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-reference).\n\nFor shared defaults, rules, and skills checked into repos or system paths, see [Team Config](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/enterprise/admin-setup#step-4-standardize-local-configuration-with-team-config).\n\nIf you just need to point the built-in OpenAI provider at an LLM proxy, router, or data-residency enabled project, set `openai_base_url` in `config.toml` instead of defining a new provider. This changes the base URL for the built-in `openai` provider without requiring a separate `model_providers.` entry.\n\n```toml\nopenai_base_url = \"https://us.api.openai.com/v1\"\n```\n\n#### Project config files (`.codex/config.toml`)\n\nIn addition to your user config, Codex reads project-scoped overrides from `.codex/config.toml` files inside your repo. Codex walks from the project root to your current working directory and loads every `.codex/config.toml` it finds. If multiple files define the same key, the closest file to your working directory wins.\n\nFor security, Codex loads project-scoped config files only when the project is trusted. If the project is untrusted, Codex ignores project `.codex/` layers, including `.codex/config.toml`, project-local hooks, and project-local rules. User and system layers remain separate and still load.\n\nRelative paths inside a project config (for example, `model_instructions_file`) are resolved relative to the `.codex/` folder that contains the `config.toml`.\n\nProject config files can't override settings that redirect credentials, alter\nhost-owned app request metadata, change provider auth, select config profiles,\nor run machine-local notification/telemetry commands. Codex ignores the\nfollowing keys in project-local `.codex/config.toml` and prints a startup\nwarning when it sees them: `openai_base_url`, `chatgpt_base_url`,\n`apps_mcp_product_sku`, `model_provider`, `model_providers`, `notify`,\n`profile`, `profiles`, `experimental_realtime_ws_base_url`, and `otel`. Set\nprovider, notification, and telemetry keys in your user-level\n`~/.codex/config.toml`; select config profiles with `--profile profile-name`\nand `~/.codex/profile-name.config.toml`.\n\n#### Hooks\n\nCodex can also load lifecycle hooks from either `hooks.json` files or inline\n`[hooks]` tables in `config.toml` files that sit next to active config layers.\n\nIn practice, the four most useful locations are:\n\n- `~/.codex/hooks.json`\n- `~/.codex/config.toml`\n- `/.codex/hooks.json`\n- `/.codex/config.toml`\n\nProject-local hooks load only when the project `.codex/` layer is trusted.\nUser-level hooks remain independent of project trust.\n\nInline TOML hooks use the same event structure as `hooks.json`:\n\n```toml\n[[hooks.PreToolUse]]\nmatcher = \"^Bash$\"\n\n[[hooks.PreToolUse.hooks]]\ntype = \"command\"\ncommand = '/usr/bin/python3 \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/pre_tool_use_policy.py\"'\ntimeout = 30\nstatusMessage = \"Checking Bash command\"\n```\n\nIf a single layer contains both `hooks.json` and inline `[hooks]`, Codex loads\nboth and warns. Prefer one representation per layer.\n\nFor the current event list, input fields, output behavior, and limitations, see\n[Hooks](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks).\n\n#### Agent roles (`[agents]` in `config.toml`)\n\nFor subagent role configuration (`[agents]` in `config.toml`), see [Subagents](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents).\n\n#### Project root detection\n\nCodex discovers project configuration (for example, `.codex/` layers and `AGENTS.md`) by walking up from the working directory until it reaches a project root.\n\nBy default, Codex treats a directory containing `.git` as the project root. To customize this behavior, set `project_root_markers` in `config.toml`:\n\n```toml\n# Treat a directory as the project root when it contains any of these markers.\nproject_root_markers = [\".git\", \".hg\", \".sl\"]\n```\n\nSet `project_root_markers = []` to skip searching parent directories and treat the current working directory as the project root.\n### Hooks\n\nSource: [Hooks](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks.md)\n\nHooks are an extensibility framework for Codex. They allow\nyou to inject your own scripts into the agentic loop, enabling features such as:\n\n- Send the chat to a custom logging/analytics engine\n- Scan your team's prompts to block accidentally pasting API keys\n- Summarize chats to create persistent memories automatically\n- Run a custom validation check when a chat turn stops, enforcing standards\n- Customize prompting when in a certain directory\n\nRuntime behavior to keep in mind:\n\n- Matching hooks from multiple files all run.\n- Multiple matching command hooks for the same event are launched concurrently,\n  so one hook can't prevent another matching hook from starting.\n- Non-managed command hooks must be reviewed and trusted before they run.\n\nHooks run at different points in a conversation:\n\n| When                              | Hooks                                                                                                                     |\n| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| During a turn                     | `PreToolUse`, `PermissionRequest`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `Stop` |\n| When a session or subagent starts | `SessionStart`, `SubagentStart`                                                                                           |\n| When the main thread ends         | `SessionEnd` (doesn't run for subagents)                                                                                  |\n\n#### Where Codex looks for hooks\n\nCodex discovers hooks next to active config layers in either of these forms:\n\n- `hooks.json`\n- inline `[hooks]` tables inside `config.toml`\n\nInstalled plugins can also bundle lifecycle config through their plugin\nmanifest or a default `hooks/hooks.json` file. See [Build\nplugins](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/plugins#bundled-mcp-servers-and-lifecycle-hooks) for the\nplugin packaging rules.\n\nIn practice, the four most useful locations are:\n\n- `~/.codex/hooks.json`\n- `~/.codex/config.toml`\n- `/.codex/hooks.json`\n- `/.codex/config.toml`\n\nIf more than one hook source exists, Codex loads all matching hooks.\nHigher-precedence config layers don't replace lower-precedence hooks.\nIf a single layer contains both `hooks.json` and inline `[hooks]`, Codex\nmerges them and warns at startup. Prefer one representation per layer.\n\nCodex can also discover hooks bundled with enabled plugins. Plugin-bundled\nhooks load alongside other hook sources and use the same trust-review flow as\nother non-managed hooks.\n\nProject-local hooks load only when the project `.codex/` layer is trusted. In\nuntrusted projects, Codex still loads user and system hooks from their own\nactive config layers.\n\n#### Review and trust hooks\n\nCodex lists configured hooks before deciding which ones can run. Before a\nnon-managed command hook can run, Codex requires you to review and trust the\nexact hook definition. Codex records trust against the hook's current hash, so\nnew or changed hooks are marked for review and skipped until trusted.\n\nUse `/hooks` in the CLI to inspect hook sources, review new or changed hooks,\ntrust hooks, or disable individual non-managed hooks. If hooks need review at\nstartup, Codex prints a warning that tells you to open `/hooks`.\n\nManaged hooks from system, MDM, cloud, or `requirements.toml` sources are marked\nas managed, trusted by policy, and can't be disabled from the user hook browser.\n\nFor one-off automation that already vets hook sources outside Codex, pass\n`--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` to run enabled hooks without requiring\npersisted hook trust for that invocation.\n\n#### Config shape\n\nHooks are organized in three levels:\n\n- A hook event such as `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`,\n  `SubagentStart`, or `Stop`\n- A matcher group that decides when that event matches\n- One or more hook handlers that run when the matcher group matches\n\n```json\n{\n  \"description\": \"Optional lifecycle hooks for this workspace.\",\n  \"hooks\": {\n    \"SessionStart\": [\n      {\n        \"matcher\": \"startup|resume\",\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"python3 ~/.codex/hooks/session_start.py\",\n            \"statusMessage\": \"Loading session notes\",\n            \"additionalContextLimit\": 5000\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"SessionEnd\": [\n      {\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"python3 ~/.codex/hooks/session_end.py\",\n            \"timeout\": 3\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"PreToolUse\": [\n      {\n        \"matcher\": \"Bash\",\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/pre_tool_use_policy.py\\\"\",\n            \"statusMessage\": \"Checking Bash command\"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"PermissionRequest\": [\n      {\n        \"matcher\": \"Bash\",\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/permission_request.py\\\"\",\n            \"statusMessage\": \"Checking approval request\"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"PostToolUse\": [\n      {\n        \"matcher\": \"Bash\",\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/post_tool_use_review.py\\\"\",\n            \"statusMessage\": \"Reviewing Bash output\"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"UserPromptSubmit\": [\n      {\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/user_prompt_submit_data_flywheel.py\\\"\"\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ],\n    \"Stop\": [\n      {\n        \"hooks\": [\n          {\n            \"type\": \"command\",\n            \"command\": \"/usr/bin/python3 \\\"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/stop_continue.py\\\"\",\n            \"timeout\": 30\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ]\n  }\n}\n```\n\nNotes:\n\n- `description` is optional top-level metadata for a `hooks.json` file. It\n  doesn't change which hooks run.\n- `timeout` is in seconds.\n- If `timeout` is omitted, Codex uses `600` seconds for most hooks.\n  - `SessionEnd` uses `1` second by default and supports up to `3` seconds.\n- `statusMessage` is optional.\n- `additionalContextLimit` sets how much `additionalContext` a command hook can\n  send to the model before Codex saves the full text to disk and sends a shorter\n  preview instead. See [Large hook output](#large-hook-output).\n- `commandWindows` is an optional Windows-only command override. In TOML, use\n  `command_windows` or `commandWindows`.\n- The `async` option is parsed, but asynchronous command hooks aren't supported\n  yet.\n- Only `type: \"command\"` handlers run today. `prompt` and `agent` handlers are\n  parsed but skipped.\n- Commands run with the session `cwd` as their working directory.\n- For repo-local hooks, prefer resolving from the git root instead of using a\n  relative path such as `.codex/hooks/...`. Codex may be started from a\n  subdirectory, and a git-root-based path keeps the hook location stable.\n\nEquivalent inline TOML in `config.toml`:\n\n```toml\n[[hooks.SessionStart]]\nmatcher = \"^compact$\"\n\n[[hooks.SessionStart.hooks]]\ntype = \"command\"\ncommand = '/usr/bin/python3 \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/session_start.py\"'\nadditionalContextLimit = 5000\n\n[[hooks.PreToolUse]]\nmatcher = \"^Bash$\"\n\n[[hooks.PreToolUse.hooks]]\ntype = \"command\"\ncommand = '/usr/bin/python3 \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/pre_tool_use_policy.py\"'\ntimeout = 30\nstatusMessage = \"Checking Bash command\"\n\n[[hooks.PostToolUse]]\nmatcher = \"^Bash$\"\n\n[[hooks.PostToolUse.hooks]]\ntype = \"command\"\ncommand = '/usr/bin/python3 \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.codex/hooks/post_tool_use_review.py\"'\ntimeout = 30\nstatusMessage = \"Reviewing Bash output\"\n```\n\n#### Turn hooks off\n\nHooks are enabled by default. To turn them off in `config.toml`, set:\n\n```toml\n[features]\nhooks = false\n```\n\nUse `hooks` as the canonical feature key. `codex_hooks` still works as a\ndeprecated alias. Admins can force hooks off the same way in\n`requirements.toml` with `[features].hooks = false`.\n\n#### Managed hooks from `requirements.toml`\n\nEnterprise-managed requirements can also define hooks inline under `[hooks]`.\nThis is useful when admins want to enforce the hook configuration while\ndelivering the actual scripts through MDM or another device-management system.\nTo enforce managed hooks even for users who disabled hooks locally, pin\n`[features].hooks = true` in `requirements.toml` alongside `[hooks]`. To ignore\nuser, project, session, and plugin hooks while still allowing administrator\nmanaged hooks, set `allow_managed_hooks_only = true`.\n\n```toml\nallow_managed_hooks_only = true\n\n[features]\nhooks = true\n\n[hooks]\nmanaged_dir = \"/enterprise/hooks\"\nwindows_managed_dir = 'C:\\enterprise\\hooks'\n\n[[hooks.PreToolUse]]\nmatcher = \"^Bash$\"\n\n[[hooks.PreToolUse.hooks]]\ntype = \"command\"\ncommand = \"python3 /enterprise/hooks/pre_tool_use_policy.py\"\ncommand_windows = 'py -3 C:\\enterprise\\hooks\\pre_tool_use_policy.py'\ntimeout = 30\nstatusMessage = \"Checking managed Bash command\"\n```\n\nNotes for managed hooks:\n\n- `managed_dir` is used on macOS and Linux.\n- `windows_managed_dir` is used on Windows.\n- Codex doesn't distribute the scripts in `managed_dir`; your enterprise\n  tooling must install and update them separately.\n- Managed hook commands should use absolute script paths under the configured\n  managed directory.\n- `allow_managed_hooks_only = true` skips hooks from user, project, session, and\n  plugin sources, but still loads managed hooks from `requirements.toml` and\n  other managed config layers.\n\n#### Plugin-bundled hooks\n\nWhen a plugin is enabled, Codex can load lifecycle hooks from that plugin\nalongside user, project, and managed hooks.\n\nBy default, Codex looks for `hooks/hooks.json` inside the plugin root. A plugin\nmanifest can override that default with a `hooks` entry in\n`.codex-plugin/plugin.json`. The manifest entry can be a `./`-prefixed path, an\narray of `./`-prefixed paths, an inline hooks object, or an array of inline\nhooks objects.\n\n```json\n{\n  \"name\": \"repo-policy\",\n  \"hooks\": \"./hooks/hooks.json\"\n}\n```\n\nManifest hook paths are resolved relative to the plugin root and must stay\ninside that root. If a manifest defines `hooks`, Codex uses those manifest\nentries instead of the default `hooks/hooks.json`.\n\nPlugin hook commands receive these environment variables:\n\n- `PLUGIN_ROOT` is a Codex-specific extension that points to the installed\n  plugin root.\n- `PLUGIN_DATA` is a Codex-specific extension that points to the plugin's\n  writable data directory.\n- Codex also sets `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` and `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` for\n  compatibility with existing plugin hooks.\n\nPlugin hooks use the same event schema as other hooks. Installing or enabling a\nplugin doesn't automatically trust its hooks; Codex skips plugin-bundled hooks\nuntil you review and trust the current hook definition.\n\n#### Matcher patterns\n\nThe `matcher` field is a regex string that filters when hooks fire. Use `\"*\"`,\n`\"\"`, or omit `matcher` entirely to match every occurrence of a supported\nevent.\n\nOnly some current Codex events honor `matcher`:\n\n| Event               | What `matcher` filters | Notes                                                        |\n| ------------------- | ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `PermissionRequest` | tool name              | Support includes `Bash`, `apply_patch`\\*, and MCP tool names |\n| `PostToolUse`       | tool name              | See [Tool coverage](#tool-coverage)                          |\n| `PostCompact`       | compaction trigger     | Values are `manual` or `auto`                                |\n| `PreCompact`        | compaction trigger     | Values are `manual` or `auto`                                |\n| `PreToolUse`        | tool name              | See [Tool coverage](#tool-coverage)                          |\n| `SessionEnd`        | end reason             | Currently only `other`                                       |\n| `SessionStart`      | start source           | Values are `startup`, `resume`, `clear`, and `compact`       |\n| `SubagentStart`     | subagent type          | Values depend on the subagent that starts                    |\n| `SubagentStop`      | subagent type          | Values depend on the subagent that stops                     |\n| `UserPromptSubmit`  | not supported          | Any configured `matcher` is ignored for this event           |\n| `Stop`              | not supported          | Any configured `matcher` is ignored for this event           |\n\n\\*For `apply_patch`, `matcher` values can also use `Edit` or `Write`.\n\nExamples:\n\n- `Bash`\n- `^apply_patch$`\n- `Edit|Write`\n- `mcp__filesystem__read_file`\n- `mcp__filesystem__.*`\n- `startup|resume|clear|compact`\n- `manual|auto`\n\n#### Tool coverage\n\n`PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` can observe more than shell and MCP calls. Most\nlocal function tools use the same hook path, so you can match their tool name,\ninspect their JSON arguments, and, for `PreToolUse`, block or rewrite the call.\n\n| Tool path                         | `PreToolUse` | `PostToolUse` | Notes                                                                                                                    |\n| --------------------------------- | ------------ | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| Shell commands                    | Yes          | Yes           | Match as `Bash`.                                                                                                         |\n| Unified exec (`exec_command`)     | Yes          | Yes           | Match as `Bash`. A later `write_stdin` poll can deliver the original command's `PostToolUse` when that command finishes. |\n| `apply_patch`                     | Yes          | Yes           | Match as `apply_patch`, `Edit`, or `Write`.                                                                              |\n| MCP tools                         | Yes          | Yes           | Match the MCP tool name, such as `mcp__filesystem__read_file`.                                                           |\n| Other local function tools        | Yes          | Yes           | Match the function tool name, such as `update_plan`. `spawn_agent` also matches `Agent`.                                 |\n| Hosted tools, such as `WebSearch` | No           | No            | These don't use the local function-tool hook path.                                                                       |\n\n`write_stdin` is transport for an existing unified-exec session. It doesn't run\n`PreToolUse` again when it sends input or polls a command that already passed\n`PreToolUse`.\n\nSome specialized tool paths can opt out of the default hook path. Treat tool\nhooks as a useful guardrail, not a complete enforcement boundary.\n\n#### Common input fields\n\nEvery command hook receives one JSON object on `stdin`.\n\nThese are the shared fields you will usually use:\n\n| Field             | Type             | Meaning                                                             |\n| ----------------- | ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n"
  }
]

## Assistant — 2026-08-05T00:40:56.534Z

첨부 설계 1,067줄 전체를 검토했습니다. Superpowers식 실행·검증 루프 관점의 핵심 리뷰는 아래 10개입니다.

1. **“Codex에는 서브에이전트가 없다”는 현재 기준으로 사실이 아닙니다.**

   현재 Codex는 multi-agent가 기본 활성화되어 있고 프로젝트별 `.codex/agents/*.toml` 커스텀 에이전트, `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent` 등을 지원합니다. 따라서 Codex의 기본 대안을 “동일 세션 역할 전환”으로 설계한 부분은 제거하고, Claude와 Codex 모두 네이티브 에이전트 어댑터를 사용해야 합니다. Orca는 두 런타임 사이의 조정에만 두는 편이 맞습니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: 같은 역할 계약으로 양쪽에서 `implementer`와 읽기 전용 `reviewer`를 각각 실행하고 single-writer가 유지됨.  
   공식 근거: [Codex subagents](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents)

2. **Codex 투영 표면도 일부 최신 동작과 어긋납니다.**

   프로젝트 `.codex/prompts/<workflow>.md`는 올바른 공유 진입점이 아닙니다. custom prompts는 deprecated이고 사용자 홈의 `~/.codex/prompts`에 있는 명시 호출 기능입니다. 프로젝트 공유 동작은 skills, `.codex/agents`, plugin/config로 표현해야 합니다. `SessionStart` 등 hooks도 현재 안정 기능이며, “버전별 미지원 가능성 때문에 AGENTS.md 인라인 bootstrap”을 기본 경로로 둘 이유가 약합니다. 다만 프로젝트 trust와 별개로 command hook의 정확한 hash 승인도 필요합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: `keel doctor`가 프로젝트 config 로드, hook 승인·실행, agent/skill 발견을 실제로 검사함.  
   공식 근거: [Custom prompts](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/custom-prompts), [Hooks](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks)

3. **로드맵은 문서 체계보다 가장 위험한 통합 가설을 먼저 검증해야 합니다.**

   현재 P0–P4는 대규모 문서·스키마·투영 체계를 먼저 만들고 실제 개발 루프를 P5에 둡니다. 실패 위험은 “두 런타임이 같은 규칙을 발견하고 같은 산출물로 협업 가능한가”에 있으므로 첫 단계는 수직 슬라이스여야 합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: `요청 → 작은 SPEC 1개 → task 1개 → 한 런타임 구현 → 다른 런타임 리뷰 → 정확한 HEAD에서 테스트 재실행 → 완료 판정`이 한 프로젝트에서 끝까지 동작함.

4. **`.agents` SSOT와 생성·링크·플러그인·hook을 동시에 도입하면 투영 계층이 과합니다.**

   “심볼릭 링크이므로 변환 버그 0”은 성립하지 않습니다. 발견 규칙, trust, 상대경로, Windows·worktree 지원, manifest와 runtime metadata 차이는 여전히 남습니다. 초기에는 공유 skill 원본과 얇은 런타임별 어댑터만 두고 동일 inode보다 콘텐츠 hash 일치를 검증하는 편이 안전합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: 양쪽 런타임이 같은 skill ID·version·hash를 보고 동일한 acceptance fixture를 통과함.

5. **문서 메타데이터만으로 `SSOT`나 `matches-code`를 증명할 수 없습니다.**

   `binds.code` 경로의 변경 시각과 문서 `updated`를 비교하는 방식은 의미적 일치를 판단하지 못합니다. CI나 hook이 자동으로 `sync`를 낮추도록 하면 정상 문서까지 수정하고 충돌을 유발할 수 있습니다. 초기에는 drift 후보를 경고만 하고, 사람 또는 검증 증거가 승격을 승인해야 합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: 실제 변경 10건 이상에서 drift 경고의 오탐·누락을 측정하고, 자동 상태 변경은 하지 않음.

6. **“fresh evidence”를 감사 가능하게 만드는 증거 스키마가 부족합니다.**

   `EVIDENCE.md`가 존재한다는 것만으로 최신 검증이 되지 않습니다. 최소한 commit SHA, dirty-tree hash, 실행 명령, 종료 코드, 시각, 환경, 결과 artifact hash를 묶고 이후 코드 변경 시 증거를 무효화해야 합니다. 그렇지 않으면 오래된 성공 로그로 완료를 선언할 수 있습니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: HEAD 또는 작업 트리가 바뀌면 이전 검증이 자동으로 stale 처리되고, 완료에는 현재 상태에 묶인 성공 증거가 필요함.

7. **`keel verify`는 결정적 오류와 추론성 경고를 분리해야 합니다.**

   스키마, 중복 ID, 깨진 링크, projection hash, 실제 테스트 종료 코드는 결정적으로 차단할 수 있습니다. 반면 AC–테스트 연결, “누락된 public API spec”, architecture glob 충돌, orphan 문서는 휴리스틱이어서 필수 차단기로 쓰면 오탐이 많아집니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: blocking check는 fixture에서 오탐 없이 재현되고, 휴리스틱은 warning과 근거 파일·라인만 반환함.

8. **모든 변경에 spec·architecture·security·performance 리뷰와 최대 5회 수정은 과도합니다.**

   Superpowers의 핵심은 task별 순차 writer, 해당 task를 보는 한 reviewer, 마지막 전체 리뷰입니다. 전문 리뷰는 인증, 데이터 변경, 성능 민감 경로 등 위험 신호가 있을 때만 추가하는 편이 낫습니다. TDD Iron Law도 동작 변경 코드에 적용하고 문서·생성물·설정에는 schema 검사나 명시적 evidence를 적용해야 합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: 소규모 변경은 task review 1회와 final verification만으로 끝나며, 전문 리뷰는 라우팅 근거가 기록될 때만 실행됨.

9. **별도 파일 relay는 Orca 협업 능력이 부족하다는 증거가 생긴 뒤 도입해야 합니다.**

   git common dir 아래 ready/heartbeat/fsync/hash 프로토콜은 cleanup, 권한, 동시성, worktree 경계 문제를 새로 만듭니다. MVP에서는 Orca 메시지와 immutable commit SHA, spec ID, review package만으로 handoff를 검증하는 편이 단순합니다.  
   최소 완료 기준: Claude→Codex→Claude handoff가 정확한 spec·commit을 보존하고 polling daemon 없이 재개됨. 유실·손상 사례가 반복될 때만 relay를 설계함.

10. **자기개선은 자동 적용보다 재현 가능한 실험부터 시작해야 합니다.**

    실패 한 번으로 lesson을 active skill이나 role에 자동 반영하면 잘못된 규칙이 빠르게 증폭됩니다. Superpowers식 skill 개선은 같은 시나리오를 skill 없이 실행한 baseline, 수정 후 재실행, 여러 반복, 사람의 승인을 요구합니다.  
    최소 완료 기준: 재현된 실패 하나에 대해 Claude와 Codex 양쪽에서 전후 비교가 있고, 사람 승인 후 versioned skill release로 승격됨.

추천하는 최초 MVP 범위는 다음 여섯 가지면 충분합니다.

- 공유 skill 원본 1개와 Claude/Codex 얇은 어댑터
- 작은 SPEC 형식 1개
- implementer와 read-only reviewer 역할
- single-writer 규칙
- 현재 HEAD에 묶인 fresh verification
- Orca를 통한 spec ID·commit SHA 기반 handoff

이 수직 슬라이스가 통과한 뒤 문서 라우터, BMAD CIS, 전문 리뷰, drift 검사, relay, 자기개선을 순서대로 추가해야 합니다.
